Here Is Every Commander Deck From 2026 Ranked From Worst To Best

📅 Jul 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Magic: The Gathering set is all wrapped up for 2026 after the launch of the Star Trek set and its four Commander precons, which have added to an already strong Universes Beyond pool this year. We do still have an unconfirmed Spider-Man Commander precon in December, but we're still waiting on more details.

That brings us to 25 unique Commander precon decks so far this year, so we're going to look back at every deck we've had the pleasure of playingand rank them from worst to best.

If you love picking up Commander precons with each new set, we've got great news, as there will be at least four precons per set next year, meaning at least 24 new precons are coming in 2027.

Updated on November 10, 2026, by Niall Walsh: We're adding the Star Trek Commander precons to this list, having had the chance to play with them over the prerelease week and put them through their paces against a number of different precons from this year.

While there are easily no terrible Commander precons from 2026 so far, one of these decks has to come in last place, and for me, it's Blight Curse.

I don't mind the Blight mechanic at all, but it's barely made an impact on Standard play, and this deck just isn't quite on the same level as many of the others released so far this year.

Blight Curse is currently worth $44.40 according to TCGPlayer.

I've played with and against this deck, and it's always struggled to keep up with the pace of the game and to have any meaningful impact. If anything, it churns away and drags games out, if only because it's the lowest threat at the table and gets treated as such.

That said, it can be quite the engine if you leave Auntie, Ool, and Cursewretch unchecked on the battlefield. Blighting creatures impact the game no matter what, either by drawing cards or by making opponents lose 1 life.

Still, it's an unremarkable deck that could use major upgrades, and some of the cards released in The Hobbit make that easier and more impactful if you want to turn it into a consistent performer at your local game night.

We take a look at the very best MTG precon decks for the Commander format.

I'm not a fan of Planeswalker-led Commander decks at the best of times, and while Lorehold Spirit can still pop off for big damage as you swing wide with Spirit tokens, it's a fairly one-dimensional deck that can be easily frustrated.

This deck is built on the Boros Flashback mechanic and general graveyard interactions. The idea is to cast spells that can be cast again from your graveyard or ones that let you manipulate your graveyard in any way. In doing so, you'll also create Spirit 3/2 tokens.

The Lorehold Spirit deck is worth $60.87 according to TCGPlayer.

The main issue I have with the deck is that any half-decent Commander player will see what this deck is trying to achieve from miles away and just reserve removal, board wipes, and graveyard exiles to repeatedly hammer down on its controller until they're left with few strategic options.

Boros tends to do better in Standard because it's one-on-one and 20 life points. You can play as aggressively as you like, and the game's limited nature often means you can overwhelm your opponent before they have a decent answer. In Commander, games naturally go on for much longer, and a deck like this gets weaker with every turn if its battlefield isn't stacked with powerful creatures and tokens.

This is one of two decks that it hurts to have so low down this ranking, but Witherbloom Pestilence just isn't as powerful as I hoped it would be, especially when this Strixhaven College is arguably the best to use in the Limited format, and has seen multiple cards cross over into the Standard meta for some fringe decks.

The deck is far too creature-heavy, with 39 in total, which is somewhat understandable given there will be a lot of sacrificing, but that's typically reserved for tokens created by instants, sorceries, and enchantments. Basically, the deck feels unbalanced, and if players know your strategy is to go all in on creatures, they'll be more aware of your combos and more keen to stop them.

The Witherbloom Pestilence deck is worth $63.52 according to TCGPlayer.

I would argue that this is one of the better decks on this list for new players, as its creature-heavy nature is good for learning about combos, synergies, and how dynamic certain creatures can be, but if you hope to make it truly competitive at your game night, it's going to take quite a few upgrades.

Admittedly, I struggle with where to rank the Turtle Power deck on this list. Looking at it from a pure power perspective, it's relatively high up. The option to run a Partner Commander is also a big plus, as is the wonderful thematic storytelling behind its design. However, it just doesn't consistently become the giant mutant engine it should be.

It's a slow starter if you don't have a curated first hand, and I would say that it's such a situational deck that even pulling generally good cards off the top of your deck can feel a little jarring when you haven't got your board set up right to play and/or protect it.

The Turtle Power deck is worth $81.67 according to TCGPlayer.

WUBRG decks are always a little tricky to judge if they're not blatantly powerful right out of the box, and because the theming of this deck is so cool, it feels disruptive to try and make upgrades to it, so it's almost a case of being a precon that you shouldn't upgrade, but yet it desperately needs it.

Ultimately, I compare this deck with the other WUBRG that has been released this year, and in doing so, it became much clearer where the two stack up against each other and the rest of this list, and Turtle Power falls into the lower half as a result.

Some aggro decks are just built better than others, and Klingon Fury has so much potential as an idea, but the end product doesn't quite live up to the bill. This feels like a deck that should be pumping out Klingon tokens and low-cost Klingon creatures, supported by burn spells and ways to increase the power of a specific creature type. This is there to some extent, but it doesn't fully commit to the idea the way Goblin Storm does, and it ends up being far slower than it should be.

A five-mana Commander doesn't help, either, and while there are ways to get some ramp to cast him on turn three, you're in "planets aligning" territory to get there reliably over and over.

It's far from a bad deck, but if you're looking for quick, clean wins, this deck isn't that, and it requires a little more work to get everything to come together and reach a dangerous board state.

We follow one Star Trek deck up with another, as Federation Fleet falls into the bottom half of decks released this year. Like so many others on this list, it's far from bad, and with the right starting hand it's brilliant, but as a consistent performer, it struggles, and the split strategy of big powerful creatures and the flying threat of Spacecrafts just clashes a little too much.

This deck relies on card draw and counter distribution, and the Seek Out ability has some huge activations that can turn a game on its head if used right, but the setup required to reach that point will likely see you identified as a threat pretty early on, and you'll rarely get to pull off any of your exciting and dangerous combos.

This deck would have benefited from being a little more adventurous with its theming and focusing a little less on Seek Out. Despite it being very easy to trigger the second card draw each turn, the triggers pile up, and it can become a chore to work through them all each time, and not all of them have any impact on your opponents.

This is the other deck that pains me to have this low, but Doom Prevails is just ripe for upgrades and never quite gets over the line to reach its potential straight out of the box.

Conniving is the name of the game with this deck, but the creatures sometimes don't mesh well with the strategy, and instead it gets a little lost, especially if you can't get good Connive cards on the board early on. Doctor Doom lets Connive benefit you no matter the outcome: when you discard a land card, each opponent takes 2 damage, and if it's a nonland card, you still put a +1/+1 counter on the creature that Connived. But without some truly repetitive Connive triggers, this isn't as powerful as it first seems.

The Doom Prevails deck is worth $93.03 according to TCGPlayer.

Black Market Connections is also a big part of the deck, and getting it onto the board early, but still struggling to put some of the combos together, just results in the deck bleeding you dry of life points while your opponents sit and laugh.

Upgrades can make this deck very good, but without that refinement, it's a little hit-and-miss, so it can't quite crack the top half of this ranking.

We're getting into the decks that are largely separated by fine margins now, with Wakanda Forever being a very good artifacts deck that just focuses a little too much on mana artifacts for my liking.

Given how T'Challa works, you won't be short on colorless mana to cast artifacts, yet a quarter of the 24 artifacts in this deck are mana generators. Beyond the basic Arcane Signet and Sol Ring, artifacts that produce mana feel wasted in this deck, so it could have leaned harder into card draw, equipment, or vehicles to really power up this strategy.

The Wakanda Forever deck is worth $84.23 according to TCGPlayer.

The instant and sorcery spells in this deck could also use some refinement. It has the expected removal and looting, but I would like to see more token generation or boosting for existing creatures to add another deadly element to the playbook.

Finally, 12 forests and 12 plains leave plenty of room for more powerful or impactful lands, which feels like an obvious starting point for upgrades.

What this deck does well, however, is stick to its core artifact-based attack, and it can become a problem very quickly if you get T'Challa churning out the Vibranium tokens as soon as possible.

Okay, so the Foundations starter decks are actually a whole lot better than I thought. Each of the five mono-color decks brings a ton of value to the table, not necessarily in the cards, but in how it teaches the basic principles of Commander as a format, while still making it incredibly fun. Oh, and each deck is competitive, too. You won't need to upgrade these decks to earn your first win in casual settings, and that arguably makes them the perfect starting block for anyone new to the format.

It's been ages since the last Commander starter decks were released, and the game has changed a lot in that time. I wasn't initially a fan of these decks being mono-color, but after reading through the decklists, it's the right play.

Taking everything into account, these are great Commander decks to start your journey with, especially for under $30, and if not for the quality of precon we've seen this year, they would have ranked much higher.

While the hype for the Hatsune Miku Secret Lair deck may have been high, when you take a step back and really look at the deck, it's nothing special, not in the same way that Goblin Storm was such an obvious win.

That said, Trostani, Selesnya's Voice is an interesting Commander who works well in token, Populate, Lifelink, and +1/+1 counter decks, and this deck list has a little bit of everything.

The Hatsune Miku deck has a market value of $447.21, according to TCGPlayer.

Taking almost the opposite approach to Goblin Storm, this Secret Lair precon is less about winning and more about having fun, so bear that in mind if you pick one up.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The 40 Best Sci-Fi and Fantasy Books of 2026 So Far

📅 Jan 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

Featuring stories about app-based love, A.I., and way more. Sound familiar?

AS WE START to inch closer and closer to the end of the year, we know you’re going to be looking to feel cozy as the weather starts to get colder. Luckily, there are plenty of exciting sci-fi and fantasy books to curl up with a big mug of something hot with and read next to a fire.

There's new releases from mainstays like Jim Butcher and Matt Dinniman (two in fact, plus an upcoming TV show). There's all manner of space operas, cyberpunk thrillers, quests to kill gods, and even a wolf who sets out to kill the human race. And toward the end of the year, Emily St. John Mandel of Station Eleven fame returns with a new hopeful futuristic tale. As summer winds down, new books will continue to offer a much-needed escape throughout 2026.

Admittedly, there's been a lot of media begging for our attention this year, like The Odyssey, the World Cup, and soon Avengers: Doomsday and Dune: Part Three. But once the adrenaline of those action-packed watches wear off, it'll be the perfect time to crack open a new read and lock into assassins dealing with an alien invasion or a botched quest to clone the founding fathers (seriously).

Below is our list of the best reads of the year no matter what kind of sci-fi or fantasy you're interested in. Keep checking back as the year continues to progress for more updates.

A group of civilians are chosen to accompany veteran astronauts on a trip to Saturn's moon, Titan. They struggle to orbit the planet, and after some struggle, they manage to get back on course and return to Earth. But the Earth they left doesn't seem to be the planet they remember.

The 18th (yes, 18th!) book in Butcher's long-running Dresden Files series finds its titular character, Harry Dresden, struggling with grief. But Chicago is struggling, his brother is seriously ill, and he's now betrothed to a vampire. He'll have to put his own feelings aside to be the wizard everyone needs him to be.

Think dating is hard right now? Maybe you'll find solace in For Human Use, where a new app helps people find love with cadavers. You read that right. And as the app gains popularity, two men behind its funding and growth are pitted against one another.

Best known for his Dungeon Crawler Carl series, Matt Dinniman is back with a standalone novel. Oliver lives on the planet New Sonora, but when Earth decides to evict the planet's tenants, they make a deadly game of it, allowing Earth's gamers to use war machines for fun. Oliver decides to try and save his home no matter what.

Leena can see the dead, but keeps her secret closely guarded. When her brother falls ill, she decides to bargain away her secret to a dangerous merchant. Her trade leads her to a mysterious estate, where she has to fight for her life to escape the merchant's grasp.

The first in a new trilogy, Tao Wong's The First Step follows a young farmer who joins a school of cultivation, and begins a magical journey filled with martial arts, Chinese mythology, and a chance at immortality.

It's post-WWI Britain, and what people hope to be a time of peace is overtaken by a new threat. Fae are taking children, and one vet decides he'll be the one to bring them back.

If you don't normally read short story collections, now's your chance. Featuring award-winning and nominated stories, this book may be the perfect read to get you out of a reading slump with tales you can finish in one sitting.

A grief support group gets dark with the newest release from horror writer Eric LaRocca. After the death of his husband, Simeon becomes enmeshed with The Wretches, whose intense methods to process the death of loved ones leads Simeon to a mysterious man who promises they can all get one last moment with a loved one for a steep price.

The graduate of a deadly academy is the protagonist of Mark Lawrence's newest novel. Rue, now an old woman, is finally at peace with her violent upbringing. But as soon as she thinks she's entered retirement, the sins of her past come back to haunt her.

Trees can drag you to your death. Artificial monsters stalk the lands. And alien men wander around town. John Maraintha is dropped on the planet Scythia and has to find a way to adapt and survive.

Fans of classic folktale retellings will enjoy this take on The Little Mermaid. Rather than following Ariel, it centers on her granddaughter, who is awash in complex politics after the assassination of her father. To save her family's legacy, she makes a deal with the famed sea-witch Ursla.

If you're a fan of The Substance, you may enjoy this new book. A young struggling writer named Maggie meets an established producer who's looking to revive her career. The solution to both of their problems is an experimental procedure–that takes Maggie's youth and gives it to the older producer–along with a $3 million payout that helps with Maggie's money troubles. But both women quickly realize that the blood transfusion is anything but a simple transaction.

Space operas are a sci-fi subgenre that never get old. When a starship's crew returns to their home galaxy after a decade away, they find the once peaceful space they knew is now on the brink of war–and they're expected to fight in it. It's up to the ship's captain to make life or death decisions that may have irrevocable impacts on her crew.

A ten-year-old girl goes on a journey with her AI toy to reach New York in this novel all about the complex relationship between man and machine.

This debut follows a billionaire who has prepared for the apocalypse and is ready to survive in his bunker with his family. But above ground, raiders are also fighting to stay alive, and aren't too happy with their new neighbors.

Earth is in ruins. A colony ship on the way to its new home keeps its original civilians in cryostasis, while their descendants maintain the vessel on its four hundred yearlong journey to a new planet. But midway through the trip, the crew of descendants starts to wonder why they're spending their lives in service of a world they'll never see.

A beloved actor in an epic fantasy franchise where much of the characters believe the world is real makes a bold choice to break out of the false reality the studio has built, and convince his co-stars to go with him.

The eighth, highly anticipated book in the Dungeon Crawler Carl series picks up on the tenth floor, where Carl and Donut try to make sense of the system AI's mysterious plans and learn more about why it calls the eleventh floor 'A Parade of Horribles'.

If you're yearning for an epic tale of political intrigue, this new Ann Leckie read is for you. The empire of Radch is dealing with internal turmoil, and when a major decision is made on the religious site called Radiant Star, that turmoil could result in irrevocable changes to everyone in the city of Ooioiaa.

All About Tatiana Gabriela, Joe Jonas’s Ex-Girlfriend

Joe Jonas and model Tatiana Gabriela have split after more than a year of dating. The pair was first linked in summer 2025, months after Jonas finalized his divorce from Sophie Turner in 2024. Days later, on January 7, People confirmed they were dating, with a source revealing that they “first started to get to know each other late last summer.”

After more than a year together, the couple called it quits. Here’s everything to know about Gabriela and her relationship with Jonas.

Gabriela is originally from Puerto Rico, per her portfolio with Killer Management, but she has made her home in New York City. She started modeling following film school and has worked with Diane von Furstenberg, For Love and Lemons, and Dolce Vita.

16 Best Automatic Watches for Men 2026, According to GQ Watch Nerds

📅 Aug 29, 2025  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A budding “watch guy” most likely starts off their collection with an assortment of quartz tickers (and let’s be clear, there’s nothing wrong with that). But as your interest in watches grows, it’s only natural for you to want something on your wrist you can brag about, which inevitably pushes you towards automatic watches.

Inside the case of an automatic watch is a weighted rotor that spins as you move your arm, a few little flicks of your wrist throughout the day keeps the internal mechanisms running long enough to keep that minute hand tick-tick-ticking across the dial.

But automatic watches also have a reputation for being finicky or costly. And while that can be true, the beauty of watches is the wide price scale in which the industry operates. From stunners for less than $250 to ones you’d have to mortgage your house (and maybe your parents’ house) to buy, here are the top automatic watches on the market today for any budget.

While not impossible to find a great watch at the $250-mark, you are going to be limited. Given the many internal pieces and complicated mechanisms needed to build an automatic watch, many brands have to charge a bit more for a quality automatic. Even so, brands like Orient and Seiko lead the way on well-made (and well-designed) options.

Orient’s range of inexpensive watches punch well above their weight-class and the Symphony III is no different. With its classic, clean design and movement that promises 40 hours of power reserve, this is a watch that’s made to be a daily driver without breaking the bank.

Timex’s Waterbury is a staple in their line-up and the extremely stylish Metrograph has an Art Deco-style case and dial that’s pared-down enough to keep things simple, but without looking boring.

The Seiko 5 is something of a cult favorite for the Japanese brand – and for good reason. It’s a perfect daily driver and a budget-friendly alternative to the Rolex Datejust. Best of all, it clocks in at just 37mm, which is the perfect case size for nearly any wrist.

As ever, Timex provides maximum bang for minimum buck with this classic "Pepsi" bezel diver.

At $500, the doorway starts to open up a bit more – but only a creak. The usual suspects like Timex and Casio, are populating this price point, Bulova and Sternglas are great alternatives who will give you a little more street cred among collectors.

Undoubtedly one of the best looking watches in the sub-$500 category, the Timex Marlin in this particular shade of green strikes the perfect balance between classic and modern.

Heavily influenced by motorsports, the Edifice is a bit of a departure from the digital, retro-charm of many mainstays for Casio. The integrated bracelet keeps it sporty, while the patterned dial makes it a little more interesting than other options on the market.

At 45mm, 200-meter of water resistance, and 42-hours of power reserve, the Bulova Marine Star ticks all the boxes for a great dive watch at a reasonable price – the two-toned bezel is just a nice bonus.

This fan favorite hits the bullseye of the style-quality-price nexus, and looks especially fetching with a bright yellow dial.

A thousand bucks is a perfectly reasonable budget to have when you want to upgrade your watch game without completely breaking the bank. Here, I’d recommend looking at smaller, or more indie brands to find some interesting designs not seen by more household names.

You’d be forgiven for not knowing their name, but Kuoe is starting to gain traction in the U.S.. The Japanese-based watchmaker produces high-quality, well-designed pieces that harken to a more classic watch style. The OLD SMITH is based on the military field watch, but with a slightly more refined design language.

A reborn grail from the Studio 54 era, with a vintage-correct 35mm case.

UNIMATIC uses the basic field watch as the springboard for their Modello Due, which strips it back to its barebones to make something a bit bolder, a bit more modern, and eye-catching.

Paulin is a Glasgow-based microbrand that’s known for colorful, unique, and whimsical watches, often playing with color to make their designs all the more interesting.

Needless to say, if money isn’t an issue, the sky’s the limit. Not all watches are created equal and buying just for the name recognition alone doesn’t always make for the best watch for the money. Instead, look for a price point that’s commensurate with the movement, design, and materials used, and you’ll never regret the decision to spend the big bucks.

Taken from a 1924 archival design, the Brunswick’s cushion case, clean lines, and mother-of-pearl dial may all feel a bit “precious”, shall we say; but this is a workhorse watch that’s destined to be a daily driver for anyone who is looking for a little eye-candy on the wrist.

The IWC Ingenieur is a masterfully made ceramic watch (and matching ceramic bracelet) that will no doubt get you a few compliments every time you wear it to the office.

Not many watches can be mistaken for a Bell & Ross watch with its larger proportions and signature square case shape. The BR-05, the brand’s most popular model, is a sportier, slightly bolder answer to Cartier’s Santos.

It’s hard to narrow down one Grand Seiko to choose, but the White Birch is subtle and versatile enough to dress up or use as your daily driver. Most impressive about this particular model is the Caliber 9SA5 movement, which promises a whopping 80-hours of power when fully wound – nearly twice that of many others on the market.

Mechanical watch movements fall into two basic categories: hand-wound and automatic. Before the invention of automatic wristwatches in the 1920s (and long before the invention of battery-powered quartz movements in the 1960s) all watches were hand-wound. That meant you needed to manually wind up your watch every day to keep it running. An automatic watch (also called a self-winding watch) doesn’t require this because it winds automatically with the movement of your body as you wear it.

Most automatic watches wind themselves via a weighted rotor, which you’ll be able to see if your watch has a see-through case back (it’s the half moon-shaped piece right on top). When you wear the watch and move around, the rotor winds a spring, which turns the hands, date wheel and everything else that moves on a watch.

To keep your watch from stopping when you’re sitting still (or when you take it off at night) automatic movements also store excess energy and slowly release it over hours or days while the rotor is stationary. This is called a “power reserve,” and it typically ranges from 40 to 80 hours, with some going as high as a week or more. They’re useful if you don’t wear a watch every day, and don’t want to have to reset the date and time every time you put it on—particularly with annual or perpetual calendar models that can be complicated to set.

It’s not a downside, per se, but they do require some upkeep. Unlike a quartz watch that requires nothing more than a new battery every few years, a mechanical watch is a finely-tuned machine consisting of many tiny, precisely calibrated parts. Watchmakers have made huge advances in shock-and waterproofing over the years, but there’s still only so much abuse a mechanical movement can take. And that’s the thing: repairs to automatic watches can be expensive.

If you stick to the manufacturer’s recommended service schedule (it’ll need an overhaul and lube every 4 to 10 years, depending on the brand and model) and don’t make a habit of wearing it in the sauna, you’ll probably be fine. Whether it’s a $200 Amazon special or a $200,000 grand complication, automatic watches are designed to be worn, so the best way to take care of your watch is to wear it as much as possible.

Style is subjective, we know—that’s the fun of it. But we’re serious about helping our audience get dressed. Whether it’s the best white sneakers, the flyest affordable suits, or the need-to-know menswear drops of the week, GQ Recommends’ perspective is built on years of hands-on experience, an insider awareness of what’s in and what’s next, and a mission to find the best version of everything out there, at every price point.

Our staffers aren’t able to try on every single piece of clothing you read about on GQ.com (fashion moves fast these days), but we have an intimate knowledge of each brand’s strengths and know the hallmarks of quality clothing—from materials and sourcing, to craftsmanship, to sustainability efforts that aren’t just greenwashing. GQ Recommends heavily emphasizes our own editorial experience with those brands, how they make their clothes, and how those clothes have been reviewed by customers. Bottom line: GQ wouldn’t tell you to wear it if we wouldn’t.

We make every effort to cast as wide of a net as possible, with an eye on identifying the best options across three key categories: quality, fit, and price.

To kick off the process, we enlist the GQ Recommends braintrust to vote on our contenders. Some of the folks involved have worked in retail, slinging clothes to the masses; others have toiled for small-batch menswear labels; all spend way too much time thinking about what hangs in their closets.

We lean on that collective experience to guide our search, culling a mix of household names, indie favorites, and the artisanal imprints on the bleeding-edge of the genre. Then we narrow down the assortment to the picks that scored the highest across quality, fit, and price.

Across the majority of our buying guides, our team boasts firsthand experience with the bulk of our selects, but a handful are totally new to us. So after several months of intense debate, we tally the votes, collate the anecdotal evidence, and emerge with a list of what we believe to be the absolute best of the category right now, from the tried-and-true stalwarts to the modern disruptors, the affordable beaters to the wildly expensive (but wildly worth-it) designer riffs.

Whatever your preferences, whatever your style, there's bound to be a superlative version on this list for you. (Read more about GQ's testing process here.)

8 Best Hotel Mattress Picks for a Five-Star Staycation (2026)

📅 Aug 26, 2025  |  Category: Travel  |  Read Source →

The hotel mattress probably isn't on your vacation itinerary, but it can make or break the trip. A lumpy, unsupportive bed can derail even the most carefully planned getaway. A really good one, though, might become an unexpected highlight—the kind that has you looking forward to bedtime and quietly checking the mattress label before checkout.

Recreating that experience at home doesn't require another plane ticket. Some of our favorite properties, including design-forward waterfront retreats and charming bed-and-breakfasts, put just as much thought into their mattresses as they do their interiors. Many turn to brands like Saatva, Ostermoor, and Casper to outfit their guest rooms; others, like the W Hotels, have signature mattresses you can purchase directly. We've rounded up our favorites, such as options with plush pillow tops, supportive coils, and pressure-relieving foam, to help you bring home the best part of your stay. The room service, unfortunately, is still up to you.

For consistency, all the prices reflect queen-size mattresses.

A stay at the Ritz-Carlton is typically reflected by its refined, elegant bedrooms. The property’s eponymous bed invites the same feeling. At 14 inches, it’s a higher-profile mattress that can help make the bed the centerpiece of the room. One of the standout qualities is its coil system, as it includes 900-plus individual springs for great edge support to help keep the sides from sagging as well as promote motion isolation—a necessary factor for couples who don’t want to sense each other’s every move.

Serta is one of the most well-known mattress manufacturers for high-end hotels, such as the Waldorf-Astoria. While many luxury properties feature custom beds from the namesake brand, it doesn’t mean you can’t shop at popular retailers such as Amazon. You’d never guess it, but this premium bed is priced under $500 (making it the most affordable on our list). While Serta offers memory foam and hybrid versions of its beds, this pillow-top innerspring mattress has been a favorite in our shopping writer Julia Harrison’s space, and she says it lives up to its “Five-Star” name.

It’s filled with three zones of coil support that keep her free of back and neck problems, and the quilted cover gives her something soft to dream about each night. “I’m really not one for pomp and frills in my mattress—I’m a firm believer that a good mattress has very little going on besides a somewhat plush top to keep you from feeling any sort of spring mechanism going on inside,” she says in her mattress review. “I instantly slept well on this mattress I think because it’s quite simply: a great mattress.” Bonus: It’s a mattress-in-a-box, so it can fit easily through narrow doors and hallways once it’s delivered, and inflate to its full size in less than a day’s time after unboxing, according to Harrison. Just in case you’re on the fence, it comes with a 100-day sleep trial so you can really see if it’s right for you before committing.

The Six Bells Countryside Inn opened its doors in the bucolic Hudson Valley in 2025, and it’s already earned two Michelin Keys. In each of the 11 carefully and whimsically designed bedrooms at this retreat, you’ll find handcrafted mattresses and bedding from New York-born brand Ostermoor. Dating back to 1853, Ostermoor’s mattresses have a storied history. The beds have innovated to foam and pocketed coil inner layers and designer covers in recent years, and we can prove they’re worth the splurge.

“From the moment I plopped down on the mattress, I was amazed by how comfortable and supportive it was,” shares contributor Zoë Sessums. “It strikes a perfect balance between spring and cushion—just the right amount of give without ever feeling too soft. I consistently wake up feeling rested and supported.” Firm without feeling rigid, this mattress looks as good as it feels, too. “The tufted top, the blue and white ticking pattern, the hand-stitched details—it feels like a piece of luxury furniture, not just a place to sleep,” Sessums adds, later writing, “I would recommend this to someone who values design and craftsmanship as much as comfort.”

Saatva has found its way into charming bed-and-breakfasts and boutique retreats across the country, including Pasadena's historic Bissell House (an 1887 Victorian filled with antique furnishings) and the Cornell Inn in the Berkshires (known for its colorful, individually designed guest rooms). You can bring that same inviting comfort home with the Saatva Classic, which comes with a plush three-inch Euro pillow top, a responsive dual-coil system, and targeted lumbar support. “It’s firm (which I prefer), regulates temperature well, and is extremely durable,” our tester Erin Kaplan, vice president of communications at Conde Nast, says. “I alternate between sleeping on my side and sleeping on my stomach, and this mattress is great for both positions.”

Choose from three firmness levels—plush soft, luxury firm, or firm—, eight different sizes, and two mattress heights to find your ideal fit. Feeling extra? Saatva also offers two adjustable bases that elevate your head and feet at the touch of a button, including a premium Plus model for an even more indulgent breakfast in bed. A 365-night trial gives you plenty of time to sleep on your decision before committing.

At 1 Hotel Brooklyn Bridge, even the beds have a view. Sweeping views of the Manhattan skyline set the scene, with reclaimed wood floors, rich leather headboards, and locally crafted details like crate-inspired closets and Fresnel glass light fixtures bringing warmth and character to the rooms. With custom Keetsa mattresses in every room, you might find yourself hitting snooze despite the spectacular view.

Back at home, the Keetsa Pillow Plus offers its own take on hotel-worthy comfort. It's something of a Goldilocks mattress: not too soft, not too firm, but just right. A three-inch pillow top combines body-contouring memory foam with responsive comfort foam to help cushion pressure points, and a soft, moisture-wicking cover designed to keep overheating at bay. Individually wrapped coils provide cushioning and support while minimizing motion transfer, so even if your bedmate tosses and turns, you can stay blissfully unaware.

When husband-and-wife duo Ely and Danielle Franko transformed a dated Catskills ski hostel into The Henson, a 16-room boutique hotel, they had a pretty good idea of what makes a great night's sleep. The couple painstakingly restored the property themselves, filling its rooms with vintage furniture, locally sourced finds, and hand-applied wallpaper. But when it came to mattresses, they didn't have to look far for inspiration: They chose the same Casper Dream Hybrid they sleep on at home. In fact, they loved it so much that they insisted on king-size mattresses in every room, even the smallest ones. The hybrid design combines responsive springs with breathable foam and three zones of support that help keep your spine aligned. Its medium feel is meant to offer a little give without leaving you sinking into the mattress, making it a crowd-pleasing choice for your own guest room. After all, the best hosts know a good night's sleep is part of the hospitality.

Hot sleepers, here’s a mattress for you. The Four Season’s signature mattress features a couple of core cooling bed qualities: proprietary cooling gel foam layers with channeled designs that promote airflow throughout the mattress, as well as a patented DualCool top layer that draws heat and moisture away from the body and keeps the bed fresh with its antimicrobial properties. The cooling mattress is designed for sleepers who need extra structure on their bed. This posh number has targeted support for the lower back area and zeroes in on alignment regardless of your sleep style.

The W Hotels are widely popular because of the bold design across the properties worldwide. To mirror this aesthetic, you can cover the brand’s mattress with colorful bedding to let your personal style shine. When it comes to the bed itself, it is defined by a plush top quilted damask design to ease side sleepers into a peaceful slumber. It’s made for people who need extra support too, though, including an individually-wrapped coil system, five-zone support system, and comfort layers for added cushioning and pressure relief.

Which Kind of Mattress Do Five-Star Hotels Use?

Most luxury hotels use hybrid mattresses in their suites to strike the right balance of softness and support, thanks to pocketed coils and layers of foam. These are also typically medium-firm mattresses since a plush mattress can be uncomfortable for some sleep positions like stomach sleepers. Ultra-firm beds are often too stiff for side sleeping and may not align with guests' expectations who are looking for soft mattresses to sink into on their vacations.

If you want to refresh your mattress without the hefty price tag of buying a new one, a mattress topper or featherbed could be a solid transitional pick. The Ritz-Carlton and W Hotels promote one made with white duck feathers, Mandarin Oriental’s exclusive topper comprises white goose feathers, Waldorf Astoria beds feature a down-alternative fill, and Marriott’s selection is polyester.

There’s no one-size-fits-all here, so it really comes down to your preferred loftiness and weight. Duck and goose feathers are heavier than others fills, though duck is slightly less fluffy and won’t trap heat as well. Down-alternatives, like polyester, are more allergen-friendly and “a good option for those who prefer to be vegan in their home,” Danielle Trotter, the former textiles technical designer at West Elm, previously shared.

What Is the Thickness of a Hotel Mattress?

Hotel mattresses usually average 12 inches in thickness. This makes it easier to hop in and out of bed, especially when using a box spring that can add extra height. Plus, it prevents the need for deep pocket sheets.

How Can I Recreate the Hotel Bed Experience at Home?

We’ve explored the entire topic on how to make your bedroom feel like a hotel, and aside from finding a new bed with a medium-firm feel, we’ve learned that percale sheets and down pillows are pretty much a must to complete the setup. When it comes to the former, hotel sheets are usually made with organic cotton percale because the fabric looks and feels crisp, giving that refined aesthetic a boutique stay is known for. Usually a 200 thread count is a good rule of thumb to look out for since this means there’s plenty of airflow between the threads for a breathable feel. Hotel pillows are usually filled with down feathers since they’re so lofty and soft, but a down alternative is just as good in case you’re an allergy-prone sleeper (just look for materials like hypoallergenic gel fibers since they’re known for being squishy without falling flat, just like a feather-filled pillow).

What Is the Difference Between Memory Foam and Latex?

Traditional memory foam has a body-contouring effect that allows your body to sink into your mattress, reducing motion transfer (a must for bedmates!). Meanwhile, latex foam offers more responsiveness (or bounce) and overall support. As Neslihan Ozdemir, founder of Girl on the Mattress, previously told AD, latex also holds up well over time and is temperature-regulating. Latex foam mattresses (and some memory foam options) are certified organic, making them eco-friendly foam picks, too.

Known as the most common mattress type, innerspring mattresses feature a bed of coils. There’s quite a range to these mattresses: Basic versions have a grid of steel coils, while premier options have a pillow top and pocketed coil technology, meaning each coil is individually wrapped to help with motion isolation. Ozdemir added that innerspring is best for people who sleep on the edge of the bed or are heavier in weight. If you prefer a more plush mattress, but still want the support of those coils, consider a hybrid mattress, which is layered with soft foam.

The Best Nontoxic Sunscreens, According to Dermatologists

📅 May 01, 2025  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Are the best nontoxic sunscreens just marketing speak? In 2026, SPF formulas labeled as “clean” or “nontoxic” are in demand—and according to dermatologists, branding in this category isn’t mere greenwashing. “While the term ‘nontoxic’ is not strictly regulated, it’s often used to describe sunscreens that avoid certain chemical filters like oxybenzone, homosalate, and octinoxate,” says Marisa Garshick, MD, a board-certified dermatologist in Manhattan and New Jersey. “Some consumers prefer this, due to concerns about irritation, allergies, or environmental impact.”

As top brands meet demand and roll out clean formulas with modern technology, nontoxic sunscreens are taking on an increasingly elegant feel, in the vein of Korean sunscreen formulas—so you can rest assured, heavy, white-cast-ridden mineral SPFs are mostly a thing of the past. Another way in which brands find a point of differentiation in the oversaturated market, as Dr. Garshick points out, is by diversifying with skincare-grade ingredients that boast benefits beyond sun protection alone. She also notes that antioxidants and hydrating ingredients, such as hyaluronic acid and glycerin, are becoming the norm in nontoxic sunscreens.

Which formulas are getting the sun-protection job done—and then some? Ahead, find the nontoxic sunscreens that dermatologists most recommend.

“There is no formal definition that qualifies something as nontoxic, but when it comes to sunscreens, it generally refers to those that don’t contain ingredients like oxybenzone or other chemical filters,” Dr. Garshick explains.

Aparovich, whose role as a biochemist at OnSkin involves safety analysis of cosmetic formulas, dives in deeper. “Nontoxic refers to a sunscreen containing no components that can be potentially detrimental to human health from the overall formulation perspective. It means that neither UV filters nor any auxiliary ingredients—such as stabilizers, emulsifiers, film-forming agents, preservatives, or fragrances—can induce known or suspected hazards, including skin irritation and sensitization, allergic reactions, endocrine disruptions, genotoxicity, and more,” she explains.

While it’s commonly thought that, in sunscreens, nontoxic refers to mineral sunscreens only, Aparovich dispels the myth. “From a human-safety standpoint, sunscreen formulas may include both mineral (physical) and/or organic (chemical) filters, with no adverse effects associated with them,” she stipulates.

Are nontoxic sunscreens as good as traditional sunscreens?

“Mineral or nontoxic sunscreens are just as effective as traditional sunscreens when used correctly,” says Dr. Day. “For my patients with eczema or psoriasis, I actually prefer them. They’re less likely to cause stinging, burning, or flares and provide excellent broad-spectrum protection.” Just like any sunscreen, she says, nontoxic ones need to be applied generously and reapplied often, especially after sweating or swimming.

Overall, Aparovich advocates looking for a sunscreen formula suited to an individual’s skin type. “People with oily, combination, or acne-prone skin should opt for products free from comedogenic ingredients to avoid clogging pores and triggering inflammation, blackheads, or breakouts,” she says. These ingredients include cacao, palm, coconut, and wheat-germ oils and chemicals like acetylated lanolin alcohol, isopropyl myristate, isopropyl linoleate, isopropyl isostearate, isopropyl palmitate, myristyl lactate, myristyl myristate, laureth-4, oleyl alcohol, and ethylhexyl palmitate.

“These are the ingredients with the highest comedogenicity rate,” she says. People with dry or sensitive skin should avoid harsh alcohols, such as alcohol, alcohol denat, ethanol, or SD alcohol, Aparovich adds. “These ingredients can compromise the skin’s barrier and cause irritation.” Additionally, she notes that an ideal nontoxic sunscreen should provide broad-spectrum protection to shield the skin from UVA and UVB rays and help reduce the risk of complex DNA damage in skin cells.

“Another key point is sunscreen’s sun protection factor, or SPF,” she says. “It indicates how effectively sunscreen can protect your skin from UVB rays, the ultraviolet light associated with sunburn.” Aparovich recommends choosing sunscreens with at least SPF 30 but ideally SPF 50, especially when the UV index is three or higher.

“Technically, there are sunscreens that claim to be 100% natural, but that term isn’t regulated,” Dr. Day says. “What matters more is that the product is clinically tested, well tolerated by sensitive skin, and free of known irritants.”

What ingredients should you avoid in sunscreen?

“Three of the most hazardous chemical filters to avoid are oxybenzone (benzophenone-3), homosalate, and octinoxate,” says Aparovich. “Studies have shown oxybenzone to be a common allergen, as well as an irritant to the eyes and skin, and a potential endocrine disruptor.” She adds that homosalate and octinoxate are linked to possible adverse effects on the endocrine system and that all three ingredients have been shown to have a negative impact on marine life and coral reefs. Additionally, Aparovich recommends avoiding formaldehyde-releasing preservatives like imidazolidinyl urea, Quaternium-15, and diazolidinyl urea, along with fragrances classified as allergenic, such as cinnamal, citral, farnesol, coumarin, eugenol, geraniol, and others. “These chemicals can trigger allergic reactions and skin irritation,” she notes. Dr. Garshick recommends seeking paraben-free products, as the ingredient can irritate some skin types.

When we test and review a product, we take a holistic approach to deliver well-rounded product recommendations. First, we lean on Vogue’s vast network of experts—from board-certified dermatologists to celebrity estheticians—to gain professional acumen on the industry’s standout products, ones these specialists would actually use on their clients. We pair their expertise with our editorial best practices to curate the thoughtful edits you read on our site.

As it relates to nontoxic sunscreens, we selected the best based on the following characteristics: ingredients, SPF type, texture, packaging, finish, and the skin-care concerns they address. To do this, we paired our own personal tests of each formula with expert guidance and reviewer insights to determine which we would recommend to you.

How To Start And Complete Arena Quests In Monster Hunter Wilds

📅 Apr 05, 2025  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Here's how to access arena quests, what they are, and what you'll get as rewards for doing them in MH Wilds.

The Monster Hunter series revolves around stalking your prey through lush environments, chasing them from area to area while scavenging for natural resources. However, sometimes you want your quarry to stand their ground, and Capcom developers are more than willing to cater to this play style with arena quests.

Introduced in Monster Hunter Wilds in Title Update 1, these missions shed the title's open-world focus in favor of contained bouts with the titular beasts. While these quests may seem like a walk in the park at first (after all, the monsters can't run away), you'll need to adapt to new equipment and skills if you wish to make it out alive.

Arena quests can be found in the new Grand Hub area, also introduced in Title Update 1. After speaking with Tetsuzan, you'll be able to enter the Hub by using the Peaks Back Gate in Suja.

You can also fast travel directly to the Grand Hub through the map screen.

After arriving, make your way to Ricardo at the Arena Quest Counter, positioned in the middle of the Hub. He can be found right next to Lotta, who you can use to start regular missions. Speak with him, and you'll be able to post an arena battle for you and your party.

Here's every monster in Monster Hunter Wilds!

Arena quests seem like they would be incredibly easy, considering both you and the monster are confined to an enclosed space. You won't spend any time chasing after them, and they won't be able to escape your offensive onslaught.

You can also activate the fence in the middle of the battlefield, butting the area in half and further limiting mobility.

However, you won't be operating with your normal gear. Arena quests force you to adopt preset armor and weapon combinations before starting the fight. That said, this is also a great opportunity to get some practice in with stuff outside your traditional wheelhouse.

Your pouch will also be completely different, meaning you'll only have access to the items the game gives you.

Each quest will give you a handful of equipment options; peruse the weapons and skills to find the best option for you.

Hunters are given specific equipment and item loadouts.

Hunters are allowed to use their own equipment and items.

Hunters are given specific equipment and item loadouts.

After slaying your chosen monster, you'll be rewarded with their corresponding coins. These can be spent at the forge with Gemma, where she can make brand-new armor sets. You may also earn pendants, which can be used for cosmetic customization.

Your quest rewards are based on how quickly you kill your target, so don't hold back.

Event Quests are limited in Monster Hunter Wilds, so make sure to take them on as soon as you can.

How To Use A Sword And Shield In Monster Hunter Wilds

📅 Mar 02, 2025  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The Sword and Shield make for a basic weapon set that is easy to learn and very rewarding to master in Monster Hunter Wilds.

With 14 different weapons to try, all of which are complex and viable, Monster Hunter Wilds offers a huge variety of gameplay styles to experience. You might be drawn to a ranged option to help keep your distance from the beasties, or maybe something complex and impressive-looking, like the Switch Axe.

If you're here, though, you might see the appeal of the humble Sword and Shield, which is an effective, user-friendly, monster-unfriendly weapon that is perfect for beginners but isn't as one-note as you might initially think. There's a lot more to this weapon than you might initially think.

Versatility is the watchword when it comes to the Sword and Shield. A skilled user of this weapon set will be able to dart around monsters easily, know when they don't need to dart around them, and deliver flurries of attacks with nimble grace.

You can see the specific Sword and Shield controls for your platform by going into Settings > Info > Play Guide > Weapon Controls > Sword and Shield.

The basic attacks of this weapon are speedy, if rather short-range. The attacks come out very quickly and are also very reactive to any movement inputs — you will be very mobile while attacking, and can even attack against some monster attacks if your timing is keen.

While this is a massive boon, it's also important to be aware of your movement inputs during a combo, as it's very easy to accidentally end up out of range thanks to some hesitancy or subconsciously moving your hunter away from the big dangerous monster in front of you.

A key aspect of the Sword and Shield is its ability to inflict both severing and blunt damage. Attacking using the Circle/B button while moving will have your hunter swinging with their shield, rather than their sword. These shield smacks deliver blunt damage that, if they hit the enemy monster's head enough, will eventually cause a knockdown.

Learning to weave shield bashes into your combos whenever you have access (and the safety!) to reach your foe's head is a great way to improve your monster hunting efficiency.

The second big selling point for this weapon is the fact that you don't have to sheath your weapon to use items, such as Potions or Antidotes. Even your Whetstone can be used with the weapon drawn.

To do this, you'll first need to hold R2/RT to raise your shield, and then press the item-use button. Using the item-use button in any other situation will just lead to you sheathing your weapon like normal.

Holding down R2/RT while using this weapon will have your hunter raise their shield in a defensive position. You can attack from this position with some low mobility but safer positions, but the main use of this technique is to reduce the damage from an incoming attack.

It's important to note that the shield this weapon provides is not the big hefty shield that other weapons might have — don't expect this shield to be able to defend against a monster's strongest attacks. Use it when you're up against light attacks, such as a Rompopolo's light jabs.

The Charge Blade is one of the more complex weapons in Monster Hunter Wilds. Here's how to master it.

While simple and easy to learn, there are some great ways to really level up your Sword and Shield gameplay, beyond just using the best Sword and Shield weapons in the game. This weapon is capable of some neat tricks.

The Sword and Shield has access to two attacks that require some charging to be used to their fullest efficacy.

The Charged Chop is a really great way to deal a ton of damage to a focused area, which is great for inflicting wounds or cutting tails. It's quite quick for a charged attack, too, so you don't necessarily need a huge opening in a monster's attack patterns to execute it.

Sliding Swipe (which is performed by hitting Triangle/Y while blocking and pressing the directional button) can also have the hunter launched into the air if they slide into a wall or ledge. Use this to your advantage with positioning and getting some aerial attacks into a fight!

But also be wary of this aspect of the ability, as getting launched into the air when you're not expecting it can be disorienting. We recommend practicing this move in the Training Camp before taking it out for a real hunt.

Not all weapons are suited to elemental affinities or status ailments, but the Sword and Shield is particularly great. This is due to the weapon being very good at delivering a very high number of attacks in a short period of time. This is especially true for status ailments.

If you're a dedicated Sword and Shield user, to the neglect of any other weapon types, a good strategy is to have two different Sword and Shields as your primary and secondary weapons. You could either stock two elements, two status ailments, or either a status or element and a strong non-elemental option. You'll have really great flexibility with this without having to sustain your skills in more than one weapon type!

This weapon's Focus Strike (which is used to destroy monster wounds and deal a lot of damage) is, shockingly enough, flexible. The initial strike into a wound (performed by aiming with L2/LT and then pressing R1/RB) will anchor your hunter onto the monster.

You can then follow the Focus Strike with either a slashing attack with Triangle/Y that deals tons of damage, or a blunt attack with Circle/B that also launches the hunter into the air and allows for a follow-up attack.

Learning when to use each variation of these attacks is a good way to really level up your gameplay with this weapon, but we've found that there's often no downside to sticking with the slashing attack variants.

There's nothing like wading in with a massive greatsword in Monster Hunter Wilds.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

3 Best Walking Pad Treadmills for Homes and Offices (2026)

📅 Feb 04, 2025  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Unlike bulky and almost always noisy treadmills, the best walking pads are slim (often portable) machines that can slide under your standing desk or fit anywhere in your home, letting you rack up steps while working, binge-watching, or just avoiding human interaction. Sitting at your desk all day is literally a pain. It’s terrible for your posture and takes a toll on your physical and mental health, gradually morphing you into a hunched-over keyboard gargoyle. Theoretically, you could take walking breaks away from your screen, but even then, you’re still spending a lot of the day on your butt.

I’m also convinced that walking pads make me more productive. Not only do you crush your daily steps, but regular use also boosts your energy levels, mood, and cardiovascular health. Walking on an incline also strengthens and tones your lower-body muscles, so you can even get a decent workout in. There are tons on the market, offering varying speed capabilities, extra features like built-in desk platforms and wheels, and durability. Our remote testers clocked serious hours walking, working, and sometimes jogging to find the best walking pads worth the space and money.

For more WFH gear recommendations, check out Best Standing Desks, Best Laptop Stands, and Best Office Chairs.

Updated July 2026: I’ve added a walking pad to avoid after recent safety recalls. Product information, prices, and links were also updated.

Most walking pads have similar capabilities, but the Urevo CyberPad stands out. It offers a 14-percent incline, which is one of the highest inclines on the market. WIRED contributing reviewer Kristin Canning loved that she could tackle steeper walks to get in a real workout without sacrificing her work productivity. She’s confident in the product’s durability, thanks to its solid feel underfoot and comfortable belt. It was ready to use right out of the box and painless to set up. It weighs about 71 pounds, so moving it around isn’t fun, but the wheels help.

You can control the CyberPad (and the colorful lights along the side rails) with its Bluetooth remote and app. As long as you open the app at some point during your walk, it’ll log your workout data from the machine, so you can keep track of daily step counts over time. You can also view your current workout metrics on a screen at the front of the machine.

The speed tops out at 4 mph, and the incline stops at level nine, which can be either a 9- or 14-percent incline, depending on how you position the machine’s feet. Unfortunately, the incline capability makes it difficult to store under furniture. Here’s the full review.

This is a much longer and heavier walking pad; it’s 57 inches (32.5 inches when folded), which means it’s a good choice for taller folks or anyone who has a long stride. The deck feels stable, though not as springy or as padded as the Sperax. I like the level of control and the tracking capabilities of this device more than the Sperax, though. You can adjust the speed via either the remote or the app, and you can use the pad without the app. You can check your time, speed, distance, calories, and steps on the large display.

However, this machine was a bit of a pain to set up. Getting the Bluetooth remote and KS Fit app to connect was a struggle. I also don’t like that when you first start using the machine, you have to spend time walking at slower speeds before you can “unlock” the upper end of the range. The app itself is a little wonky; if you don’t start a workout with the app, your progress won’t be tracked or logged, and it doesn’t sync later. It also saves each walking session separately, so if you take a break, you start from zero again.

The app also offers workouts, which auto-adjust the speed to your pace via sensors under the belt. It felt awkward, since I couldn’t figure out how to slow the pace without the device totally stopping. You switch between modes within the app or on the display, and you can stop and start the machine using the app or remote. I like to use the machine in manual mode, and I tend to use the app more than the remote, because the remote connectivity is inconsistent.

The machine feels like it will have a long lifespan. Its heft makes it harder to move around than the Sperax, and while the folding capability is a win in general, if you plan to slide this under something, make sure the folded height (5.4 inches) will work. Here’s my full review. —Kristin Canning

The Urevo Strol 2S Pro doubles as a treadmill and feels shockingly stable underfoot for how relatively lightweight it feels. I’ve tested several affordable treadmills, and they tend to feel wobbly at higher speeds, but this one remained firmly in place even as I ran on it at top speed and incline (7.6 mph and 9 percent). That top speed isn’t fast enough if you’re looking to really sprint, but it’s definitely ample for a good workout.

If you want to use the treadmill as a walking pad, you can keep the handrail down, which limits the top speed to 4 mph (though you can still use the full incline functionality). There’s a display on the front of the machine that cycles between time, distance, calories, incline, and speed. To access faster speeds, you have to put the handrail up, which requires you to use a hex tool to tighten it into place. The handlebar has a display as well, and you can control the machine’s basic functions from there. There’s also a tablet holder you can attach.

The machine was very easy to set up, and the remote and app connected to it right away. This app was my favorite of those I tested—it was intuitive, showed the info I wanted to see in my profile (like daily step counts), and synced well with the machine. There are some wonky translations, but nothing that bothered me or made it hard to use. As long as you open the app and connect to your treadmill at some point during your walk (even if you start and control the machine outside the app), it will log your workout data. (This isn’t the case for many of the options on this list.) In the app, you can use the Quick Start function to walk freely and adjust the settings from your phone, or you can do a virtual walk with iconic scenery.

I really appreciated how quiet this treadmill was. Overall, it seems like a durable machine that offers great value, especially with the speed and incline ranges. —Kristin Canning

This walking pad had the most intuitive setup of any on this list, most likely because of its lack of a companion app, which oftentimes feels redundant. Reviewer Kristin Canning was able to set it up in minutes, and the remote connected right away. It felt stable underfoot, even at a high incline. The remote allows you to take on 30-minute, preprogrammed walking workouts, too, where the machine auto-adjusts the speed throughout (though not the incline). Otherwise, you can use it in manual mode and adjust the settings to your liking. Unfortunately, it doesn’t track step count like some of the other walking pads I’ve tested.

The Auto Incline Walking Treadmill offers a solid incline range, going up to a level nine, or a 12-percent incline. After the Urevo CyberPad, it’s one of the highest incline capabilities I’ve found in my testing. Canning was most impressed by how quiet it is. You can connect your phone to play music through the speakers too.

NordicTrack Commercial 1750 Treadmill for $2,500: With a top speed of 12 mph, incline and decline options, a springy running deck, fans, a 16-inch pivoting touchscreen, and two water bottle holders, this is the best treadmill tester Kristin Canning has used. Although it is a foldable treadmill and suitable for walking and hiking workouts, it’s still bulky and relatively expensive if you’re only looking for a space-saving walking pad. If you’re searching for a running treadmill, check out Canning’s guide to the Best Treadmills.

Toputure TP6 Walking Pad for $238: This is a foldable, screenless walking pad that is ideal for small apartments and spaces. It takes only a few minutes to set up and compact, but it weighs 56 pounds, so it’s not the most portable. During my second day of testing, the pad began making a high-pitched scraping noise, which likely indicated a misaligned belt. This unbearable noise would come and go throughout my two weeks of testing. The handlebars are height-adjustable, ranging from 32 to 45 inches, and they are also tilt-adjustable. Be aware of the handlebar locks while walking by, as they can catch on clothing. As a budget option, I still prefer the Sperax, which is a couple of dollars cheaper.

Urevo SpaceWalk 5L Smart Walking Pad for $400: We’ve tested a couple of Urevo walking pads, and while the SpaceWalk 5L is decent and cheaper, we still prefer the CyberPad for Home for its 14 percent incline and the Strol 2S Pro for its speed and ability to double as a treadmill. The SpaceWalk 5L has a larger weight capacity, maxing out at 300 pounds, with the same incline as the Strol 2S Pro (9 percent) and max speed as the CyberPad (4 mph). Its track length is a solid 39 inches, and WIRED contributing reviewer Michael Venutolo-Mantovani said it’s compact enough to stash underneath a couch or loveseat.

Lifesmart TM2202 3-in-1 Treadmill for $699: The 3-in-1 Treadmill gets its name from its ability to serve as a walking pad (with the rail down, the speed tops out at 3.7 mph), a treadmill (with the rail up, you can run up to 7.5 mph), and a workstation (with the rail up and desk attachment on) for multitasking. It’s easy to set up, and switching between modes is quick and simple (you just need to use a small tool to adjust the rail; the tool is magnetized, so it can live on the rail and never get lost in a junk drawer). The desk’s height won’t accommodate everyone; it was lower than I’d like, and I’m 5'4". But for quick, basic work like answering emails or sitting in on a meeting, it got the job done just fine. It also doesn’t have any incline options. —Kristin Canning

GoYouth 2-in-1 Under Desk Treadmill for $270: This is an Amazon top pick, but it’s very bare-bones for this price. There’s no app, and the remote and controls are unintuitive. The built-in Bluetooth speaker sounds unsurprisingly crappy. But it does feel great underfoot, is very quiet, and has a higher weight capacity (300 pounds) and a slightly longer belt than the Sperax above. If you’re a larger person looking for a simple, affordable walking pad, this will work for you. —Adrienne So

Egofit Walker Pro M1 for $479: This is one of the first walking pads we’ve tested, and it has a fixed 5 percent incline that might pose some storage issues (you won’t be able to just slide it under the couch). It can be controlled via an app or a remote, but it doesn’t automatically sync if you use the remote. But it’s lightweight and easy to move around your home.

Sperax Walking Pad: The US Consumer Product Safety Commission has warned consumers to stop using several Sperax walking pad and treadmill models due to the fall and fire hazards they pose. According to the CPSC, the machines can unexpectedly change speeds or come to a sudden stop, and there have also been reports of overheating and fires. The warning applies to the Pro, Q1, RM-01, and RM-02 models, but I’d avoid this brand altogether.

Size and storage: Walking pads are typically much smaller than traditional treadmills, but check the product dimensions and make sure the footprint will work for you. If you’re working with an especially small space, consider a foldable or under-desk walking pad. If you’re planning to store it under furniture or in a closet when not in use, shop for slimmer models with wheels for portability.

Belt size and track length: The size of your track is dependent on the product’s dimensions, and you should take into consideration how much space you have to work with. But, generally, I prefer wider and lengthier belts that feel more stable to walk on, especially if you’re on the taller side or have a longer stride.

Weight capacity: Walking pads aren’t as durable as treadmills and tend to have a lower weight capacity. Check the machine’s limit, and make sure it will work for you and anyone else in the household who will be using it. Per the online reviews we’ve read, walking pads are notorious for falling apart after only a few years or even a few months of use, so it might be worth getting something that’s a little more heavy-duty.

Speed range: Choose a walking pad that offers a speed range that best fits your lifestyle and goals. Most models will generally offer speeds up to 4 mph, which should cover everything from gentle strolls and brisk walks, and is ideal for home office use. There are walking pad-treadmill hybrids that offer faster paces if you’re also looking for a jog.

Adjustable inclines: Some models remain flat, but I prefer the walking pads that feature adjustable levels so that you can customize your cardio intensity as you see fit. Walking at an incline can also strengthen your lower body muscles and reduce anterior knee loading.

Metrics and display: Most walking pads have an LED display that tracks your steps, distance, time, and calories. Some models also include a remote control and/or app, which can sometimes sync your metrics from the machine, so you can check your workout data on your phone. This may not matter to you if you prefer to use your Apple Watch or fitness tracker.

Price: You can snag a solid, no-frills walking pad for under $200, but if you’re looking for a higher-end model with a surplus of features, it can cost $500+.

Our remote team members evaluated each walking pad from their own home offices, using the pads during real workdays. Testers of different heights, stride lengths, and lifestyles walked for hour-plus stretches while answering emails, taking meetings, and performing other work-related tasks; we noted how each pad felt over time and whether any annoyances surfaced with prolonged use. I’ll be testing the DeerRun Q2 Urban Smart Walking Pad and the GoPlus 2-in-1 Folding Treadmill. I’m also reevaluating Urevo CyberPad for Home.

Who Are The Clarks & Newmans In Black Doves? Crime Families Explained

📅 Dec 11, 2024  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

WARNING: SPOILERS ahead for Black Doves season 1.

The Clarks and the Newmans are two feared and dangerous London-based crime family organizations in the Netflix espionage series Black Doves. Created by Joe Barton (The Lazarus Project, Giri/Haji), Black Doves has outstanding reviews, earning a 98% Rotten Tomatoes critic score after its December 5, 2024 release date. Knightley (Atonement, Pirates of the Caribbean) leads the cast of Black Doves along with Ben Whishaw's Sam Young, best known for playing Q in Daniel Craig's James Bond films. Black Doves features a stellar supporting cast, including Sarah Lancashire's Reed, Kathryn Hunter's Lenny, and Ella Lily Hyland's Williams.

Black Doves follows public figure Helen Webb, the loving and supportive wife of Andrew Buchan's Wallace Webb, a prominent British politician. Webb is also a secret agent working for the mysterious covert spy organization known as the Black Doves, which Mrs. Reed is the head of. Reed confronts Helen over an affair she was having with a civil servant named Jason (Andrew Koji), who was shockingly shot and killed in a park from long range. Suspecting that Helen could be in hot water with a powerful criminal conspiracy headed by the notorious Clark family, Reed enlists a trusted triggerman named Sam Young for assistance.

In Black Doves season 1, the Clarks and the Newmans are two separate organized crime families who operate out of London, England. The Newmans are the first of the two crime families to be introduced in the series through a flashback involving Sam several years before the main plot of the series takes place. As one of Sam's early assignments after Lenny ordered him to take out his father, Frank, for his first hit, Sam kills three of the four Newman brothers who were starting to make a name for themselves as drug dealers. The Newman brothers were a violent group of drug dealers with quite a gang at their disposal. Young Hector launched an attack on Sam with this gang to seek revenge for his brothers. He's played by Luther Ford.

The Clarks are a much bigger crime family conglomerate out of London. They have international outreach and have established connections in several countries, including the United States. Alex Clark was the head of the Clark family mob before Sam killed her at the end of Black Doves season 1. Her son, Trent Clark, was not a prominent figure in the organization apart from being Alex's son. He appears to be of little use to Alex in the series but is the main reason why several of the deaths take place, most significantly Ambassador Chen, who he accidentally killed, and Jason, who he hired another assassin named Elmore Fitch to murder.

Alex Clark, played by Tracey Ullman, is the London crime boss and the Clark matriarch in Black Doves. Her son, Trent, is meant to be her successor in the business. Helen confronts Alex in a showdown late in the season, and Alex claims she had no part to play in the death of Jason Davies.

Hector was the boy who Sam let live, which forced him to walk away from the assassin business and get out of London for seven years. When Sam returns to London, Lenny gets a hold of him and wants him to make good on the botched hit on Hector Newman, who had grown up and expanded his own profitable drug ring. Lenny still found Hector to be a nuisance in London even after all those years and forced Sam to make good on his promise. Newman had gotten more powerful and disruptive after Sam let him off the hook.

Sam didn't pull the trigger on Hector when he had the chance because he was just a kid and that would have violated his own moral code as a hitman. Apart from Hector, Black Doves season 1 doesn't introduce any other prominent Newmans, indicating that Hector may be the last man standing in his crime family. Hector's operation was weakened after the Clarks took out dozens of his men searching for Kai-Ming, Ambassador Chen's daughter. This is why Hector doesn't kill when he has the chance and why he shows up at Michale's apartment asking to join forces at the end of the season. Hector also thanks Sam for killing his bully brothers and offers him a job.

Trent Clark is the main reason why the entire plot unfolds in Black Doves and is why Ambassador Chen, Jason, Maggie, Phillip, Stephen Yarrick, and even Elmore Fitch, were all killed. Despite his pleas for innocence and his admission of guilt in the Black Doves season 1 finale, Trent had far too much blood on his hands. In the final moments of Black Doves season 1, Sam sensed that Helen was about to take revenge on Trent for killing Jason and taking more lives trying to cover it up. Sam takes out Trent, Alex, and their bodyguard so Helen doesn't bring danger to her family. In the Black Doves season 1 finale, Sam shoots Alex and then kills Trent before Helen has a chance to pull the trigger herself. He appears to be sparing her the burden of being a killer like him. A distraught Helen says Trent was hers to kill.

There's nothing really tragic about Trent's death since he is by far the most irredeemable character in the series. The same can be said for Alex Clar, who was completely unaware that Trent was trying to cover himself by ordering hits on everyone who knew about Ambassador Chen's death. While Alex and Trent are not featured much in Black Doves season 1, and won't be included in the eventual season 2, their deaths could spell trouble for Helen and Sam in future episodes.

If anything, the Clarks and the Newmans are rival crime families with very different levels of power and scale. The Clarks are internationally recognized, while the Newmans, or at least what's left of them with Hector, are a more local clan operating in the United Kingdom. Hector Newman may have additional connections throughout Europe since his primary trade appears to be selling and distributing drugs.

One of the biggest mysteries in Black Doves season 1 is how Hector Newman came to find Kai-Ming in the first place and why she was stuck in his warehouse. Since the Clarks were looking for Kai-Ming and shot up the Newmans' warehouse, it wouldn't make sense if the two crime families were operating on the same team. It's more than likely that the Newmans and the Clarks are rival gangs, although the Clarks are much more sophisticated than the Newmans in the first season. Since Alex Clark is dead, Hector Newman could try to gain power among the Clarks or enlist some of their henchmen and resources to expand his organization in Black Doves season 2.

After discovering her lover's murder, a spy masquerading as a politician's wife joins forces with an old assassin associate to uncover the truth and seek revenge.
