Ranking the 25 Best Dribblers in World Football (2026)

📅 Aug 18, 2024  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

There is a growing feeling that fun is fading out of football. The legendary Argentine coach Marcelo Bielsa lamented the sport's supposed 'decline' in 2024, claiming that the spectacle "is becoming less and less attractive".

Manchester City's all-conquering ex-manager Pep Guardiola, a noted Bielsa disciple, has been blamed by some for this perceived drop-off. Guardiola's positional, possession-heavy system serves as the inspiration for coaches across the globe. A common misconception is that this style turns mercurial individuals into mindless cogs in a winning machine, but he has made it his mission to sign talented dribblers.

For all the doom and gloom, there is a fleet of wondrous weavers scattered across the elite level. You just have to know where to look. Here's a rundown of the players who are railing against the misdirected idea of football's decline right now.

From Lionel Messi to Ronaldinho, there have been some incredible dribblers over the years.

The latest Brazilian wonderkid to take South American football by storm, Estevao is certainly one to watch. In his debut season at Chelsea, the young winger showed several flashes of brilliance, including against Barcelona in the Champions League. Now, he's battling to make an impact under Xabi Alonso.

Fleet-footed and with an incredible change of pace that makes marking him one-v-one such a difficult prospect, Estevao has taken to the Premier League like a duck to water, and you can only imagine what he might become in the future.

One of the most exciting wingers in England, Antoine Semenyo was always destined to make it to the top and it was no surprise when Manchester City came calling.

The former Bournemouth man took little time to get going at the Etihad, scoring seven goals in 17 league games in his debut run with the side from January until the end of last season. Since then, it's become clear that he's now a key man.

Able to strike the ball with both feet and carry it long distances too - Semenyo is undoubtedly one of the best dribblers in the league.

It is a testament to Nico Paz's ability that some of the biggest clubs were after his signature before he put pen to paper at Real Madrid and headed back on loan to Como for one more season.

Paz has been lighting up the Serie A under Cesc Fabregas, and looks tailor-made to become a fan-favourite at the Santiago Bernabeu when he does eventually make the move, combining his excellent first touch with the exciting trickery that Manchester United saw first-hand in the Champions League.

If you've not heard the name Mika Godts, then go and watch his goal against NAC Breda from April 2026. It was an excellent individual effort, which summed up his talent in full. After such an outstanding campaign for Ajax, it came as no shock when the winger earned a move to PSG.

Yet to even reach his full potential, there's every chance that Godts becomes a world-beater in France, just like so many players have done before him.

The fulcrum and heartbeat of Barcelona's midfield alongside Frenkie de Jong - who himself can count himself unlucky not to make it onto this list given his press resistance - Pedri is the absolute epitome of a La Masia-educated star. Quick feet, quick thinking and ability to handle the ball in tight spaces, good luck in trying to take the ball off the little Spaniard.

Whether it's driving with the ball through midfield and setting up attacks, or receiving the ball under pressure and turning away from his man, Pedri can do it all when it comes to having the ball at his feet.

As Liverpool fell apart in the 2025/26 season and Arne Slot headed for the exit door, Luis Diaz thrived away from Anfield. Since joining Bayern Munich, he enjoyed some of the best spells of his career, acting as a classic inside forward capable of beating markers with ease and finding the back of the net.

The former Liverpool man has more than earned his place on this list and, in some ways, is unfortunate to miss out on an even higher ranking due to a slow start to the 2026/27 campaign.

One of the fastest rising English talents right now, Morgan Rogers has developed into a superb player since getting his chance at Aston Villa and then joining Chelsea.

A former Manchester City academy product, Rogers' skill as a dribbler is one of a powerhouse, bulldozing his way through the middle of the park and then timing the pass into his centre-forward.

Part of one of the most electrifying front threes in the Premier League with Cole Palmer and Joao Pedro, Rogers may even go down as one of the best signings in recent memory, despite his place as the most expensive in English football history.

The first of four Real Madrid names on this list, Arda Guler is arguably one of the most underrated players in Europe. The Turkish star is among the best creators in the world and that stems from his passing ability, as well as his ability to create space through intricate dribbles.

If anyone deserves a bigger place in the spotlight, it is the player who is quickly becoming a key man under Jose Mourinho at the Santiago Bernabeu.

There's good reason why Liverpool splashed out around £123m to sign Bradley Barcola in the summer of 2026. They set their sights on a direct, quick winger who's also capable of chipping in with goal involvements. The Frenchman is exactly that and already has a hat-trick to his name at the Merseyside club.

He does his best work in high-intensity sides who counter with speed, making manager Andoni Iraola the perfect match at Anfield. Should he reach his best form, then Barcola could surge up this list.

There were some question marks around Anthony Gordon's ability to adapt to life at Barcelona following a big-money move from Newcastle United. Since then, however, he has answered those questions in emphatic fashion, putting any doubts to bed once and for all.

He is proving to be a crucial player for Hansi Flick, linking up with the rest of Barcelona's stars to become one of the best dribblers that world football has to offer.

The conversation heated up over the accuracy of the dribbling rankings, with several participants challenging the selections and placements of players. @Emmanuel appreciated the article's writing, while voices like @stephen celebrated Ghanaian talent proudly represented on the list. Questions about omitted stars and ranking criteria were common; as @Tia Marina emphasized, By the stats you give in the freaking article, Lamine beats Dembele and Messi. 6 Best Bamboo Sheets That Mirror Silk, Tested by AD (2026)

📅 Mar 11, 2024  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If you love the slight sheen and smooth feel of silk bedding like me, my guess is that bamboo sheets are probably up your alley too. The natural material can make your bedroom look elevated, and you won’t actually have to pay for pure silk or satin.

What makes bamboo sheets so popular is that they’re also just as good for hot sleepers: They have moisture-wicking properties, are hypoallergenic by nature, and feel cool to the touch (no disrespect to linen lovers). The lightweight weave also has a lovely drape to it. For a dramatic look, especially in a dark and moody color palette like this Upper East Side family home, these sumptuous sheets can’t be beat.

I’ve put on Sleep Week for the last couple of years, where AD editors share the essentials for styling a bedroom. Needless to say, I’ve tried a lot of sheets from our roster of beloved bedding brands to match my ever-changing space. I arguably have too many sets to count, some new and some old, to give my space personality—and my colleagues can say the same. After all this research, I can confidently share this concise list of our all-time favorites in the bamboo category.

For consistency, all of the prices reflect queen sizes. However, many brands reach up to a king size.

Cozy Earth is a personal favorite of mine for bamboo sheets—the brand’s bread and butter. They’re so breathable—even when I layer with a thick down comforter on top and throw blanket at the foot of the bed when it’s cold outside or when my AC is blasting—and have deep pockets to fit my notoriously tall mattress and topper combo. My taste just so happens to overlap with Oprah’s, if you’ve peeked at her Favorite Things list recently, which feels like a good omen, too.

I’m not the only fan, this set has earned the top overall pick in this story because pretty much every Shop AD writer, editor, and contributor who’s tried the Cozy Earth bamboo sheet set stands behind it for a few reasons: It is temperature-regulating, long-lasting (even after several cycles in the washing machine), and some of the softest bedding out there. Contributor Yelena Moroz Alpert calls her relationship to Cozy Earth a “borderline obsession” because of the natural fibers and neutral colorways that give the bedding an “earthy sophistication.” Shopping editor Audrey Lee calls the sheets “super light and airy, so it’s mostly a summer staple.”

Contributor Megan Wahn adds that the set is a favorite of hers because it feels much more expensive than it actually is: “I honestly thought these sheets were silk when I first slept on them, that’s how smooth and buttery soft they feel.” I love the walnut shade since my bedroom leans more moody, but if you’re into a clean, neutral shade like 2026’s color of the year, Cloud Dancer, I recommend the white or gray color for a calm, minimal bedroom. Their shore set is another style for anyone who likes a more coastal look. But Alpert says it best: “Not sure how Cozy Earth does it but the sheets feel like velvet without being heavy.”

Quince is the luxury-on-a-budget brand I’ve become pretty familiar with over the last two years of trying their duvets, sheet sets, and even their bath towels—and the bamboo sheets don’t fall short. Breathable bedding is a must for my sleep setup, and I’ve been a little surprised by how well I sleep through the night with this set. It feels smooth and cool to the touch, but still cozy enough to bundle up with during the winter months. One of my best friends is a hot sleeper, so whenever he visits, I use these (and the matching bamboo duvet cover) to make his bed. He says these truly keep him at a comfortable temperature throughout the night, so he’s never throwing the covers off mid-REM sleep.

This is the brand I’d recommend if you want to style your bed to a tee. I have the bamboo sheets in a warm terracotta hue to give my bedroom a serene, almost desert Airbnb-like aesthetic in the summertime. There are almost a dozen neutral and vibrant earth tones to match your bedroom’s design, though. These points, on top of the sub-$150 price tag, earned them a top spot in this list. However, if you’re like me and wear oily sheet masks and use thick body butters at night, you might notice some light staining on the sheets.

Because of their glossy sheen, these Luxome sheets probably look the closest to silk out of all of the picks in this list. I have the bedding in a truffle color, but what makes this option so unique are the jewel tones available, like sapphire, that are hard to find with other brands. The generous size of these sheets (they have a 17-inch pocket depth) gives them an even more elegant drape for a glam bedroom.

The neutral tones are compelling, too. For Sleep Week this March, the Shop AD team and I explored how to design a bedroom with a narrative, so we designed rooms inspired by classic novels. One of those storybook sanctuaries took inspiration from Dune, and I think Luxome’s sheets (which happen to have the same name) have a cool, chrome-like finish for fans of futuristic Sci-Fi novels like this one. All in all, these are also soft and breathable, a welcome relief for me when I’m combating hot NYC days.

Yes, we like heritage brands that have been acclaimed for ages at AD, but we’re always on the hunt for new and budget-friendly brands that are worthwhile, too. Sijo launched an Amazon-exclusive line a few years ago that offers a luxe feel and sustainable material sourcing at a lower price point. This basic Doz by Sijo set of bamboo sheets is another staple for my sleep setup. My skin has gotten pretty sensitive over time, and I love how soothed I feel when I sink into these sheets. They have a little more of a polished texture than other picks in this list, so I glide into bed. It’s hard to tell the difference between these and other high-end picks on the market by virtue of their material; it feels like a softer, less slippery satin. The pockets aren’t as deep as Cozy Earth’s, and the colors aren’t as striking as Quince’s burgundy and terracotta options, but for the price, I say it’s still a nice and simple set to shop.

Even though contributor Blake Bakkila prefers more textured bedding like linen, she says Cariloha’s “Aloha” bedding has been a saving grace to keep her cool. “These were the perfect sheets to layer on our bed during a heat wave that rocked Southern California, and they only got softer with each wash,” she shares. After Bakkila and her husband adjusted to the supple texture, she says the “temperature-regulating bamboo” sheets have been a great choice to keep in rotation since they’re “exceptionally smooth and lightweight” with a “cooling effect.” Keep in mind that these are truly thin sheets and almost have a transparent appearance. So if you’re looking for bedding that’s a bit weightier, the Quince or Cozy Earth in this list might be a better way to go.

These sheets are made of 100% bamboo infused with what Bearaby calls “smart botanicals,” including extracts like peppermint and marine proteins. They don’t feel sticky like some silky sheets can when it’s hot outside—they’re airy and refreshing to sleep on and the minty essence only enhances the feeling of freshness. These botanicals do more than smell good—they eliminate up to 99.9% of bacteria directly within the fabric. As your heat rises, the botanicals activate to cool you while keeping the material clean. And—to answer the million-dollar question—the sheets do live up to their “second skin” name—the fabric is so soft and fluid, it feels like it's melting on my body.

Here at Shop AD, we don’t test sheets in a lab—we test them in our very own homes. Why? Because that’s where you’ll be using them too. Each of the sets in our Best Of roundups has been washed, dried, and slept on for countless nights by one of our editors to ensure that they’ve put them through the ringer. The sets are selected to reflect a variety of price points, materials, and styles, and we pride ourselves in giving each set firsthand, real-world use.

Whether you’re a bamboo bedding faithful or it’s your first time exploring the category, there are a few factors I recommend keeping in mind below.

When it comes to the world of linens, bamboo fibers are made from the bamboo plant, which requires far less water than cotton and regrows quickly, so bamboo sheets are often considered one of the more eco-friendly options available. There are few popular bamboo materials: bamboo viscose, rayon, and lyocell. All are antimicrobial, soft to the touch, and absorbent. Depending on if and what they are blended with, they can take on more linen- or cotton-like properties. Here’s a breakdown of each:

The fabric’s weave will change the texture, softness, and general propensity for wrinkling. Below, here are more details on the two most popular weaves: twill, sateen, and percale.

To keep laundry day simple, the bedding in our list simply requires a machine wash and tumble dry. We generally recommend washing on cold and drying on low, as these gentler settings can help preserve the bedding’s quality. However, be sure to check the labels on the sheets for any specific care instructions.

While all bamboo sheets do a superb job of temperature regulating, your best bet (for overall comfort plus silkiness!) is to find a 100% bamboo viscose or lyocell set—like the Cozy Earth pick—with a thread count between 250 and 350, made with a sateen or percale weave. This typically indicates a durable weave that won’t wear easily over time.

As enthusiasts for all things interiors, we recommend looking for bamboo sheet sets that come in colors, prints, and patterns that can match your bedroom’s design. So, if you lean more minimalist, try neutrals like ivory. If you love maximalist style, go big with bold designs and hues—from embroidered details to splashy colors like terracotta or butter yellow.

You can usually find a set of this bedding type between the $50 to $400 range. To get the most out of your purchase, we recommend looking for eco-friendly certifications to help make sure your sheet set has the best quality. For example, the Oeko-Tex Standard 100 applies to bedding that is free of harmful chemicals.

In essence, yes—but how much so really depends on the quality of your set. Bamboo bedding is known for being cool to the touch overall, but the weave itself will ultimately determine the breathability of the sheets. Keep an eye out for bamboo sheets with a 300 thread count weave or above, as our editors mention that these typically have the most airflow for a good night’s rest.

Is Bamboo Viscose Better Than Egyptian Cotton?

Bamboo sheets, because of the strength of their fibers, actually have better longevity than cotton sheets, and offer superior moisture-wicking and temperature regulation to cotton sheets. Bamboo, especially bamboo viscose, also brags a smoother, more soothing feel against the skin compared to cotton, which can require more washes to get the the super soft or silky feel you desire.

Yes! The nature of bamboo is hypoallergenic, making bamboo bed sheets perfect for those with sensitive skin, eczema, or anyone prone to allergies. Bamboo’s super-fine fibers inherently resist things like dust mites, mold, and mildew. Even at the molecular level, it’s a moisture-wicking material, helping you stay sweat-free while also creating an environment inhospitable for dust mites (that need moisture). The fibers are naturally smooth, too, making for a soft sleeping experience.

How Do Bamboo Sheets Compare to Linen in Terms of Softness and Durability?

In our experience, both materials are durable when the sheets are made of 100% natural fibers instead of blends, but bamboo sheets are much softer. Unless it’s stonewashed, linen is usually known to feel a bit scratchy at first and then soften with wear and care. Bamboo sheets are softer down to their microscopic makeup, as their very fibers are smoother than linen.

Every Upcoming Lord Of The Rings Movie & TV Show

📅 Nov 19, 2023  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The future of The Lord of the Rings looks bright, with several upcoming TV shows and movies set in Middle-earth currently in production. Since the original The Lord of the Rings trilogy began in 2001, the franchise's movie & TV portfolio has expanded via prequels and spinoffs. The enduring legacy of Peter Jackson's movies means rights holders are eager to produce more content derived from J.R.R. Tolkien's Lord of the Rings books, and audiences are just as eager to consume it.

As of 2026, the official Middle-earth movies and TV shows have been the animated Lord of the Rings adaptations released in the late 1970s and early 1980s, Peter Jackson's The Lord of the Rings trilogy, Jackson's subsequent The Hobbit trilogy, the theatrical anime film, The Lord of the Rings: The War of the Rohirrim, and Amazon's The Rings of Power. The lack of further material is partly because the rights to adapt Tolkien's work are split among various parties, but both Warner Bros. and Amazon currently have Middle-earth projects in their pipelines, from prequel The Lord of the Rings movies to big-budget TV spinoffs.

The next installment in The Lord of the Rings' biggest TV spin-off is The Rings of Power season 3. Seasons 1 and 2 aired on Prime Video in 2022 and 2024, respectively, with both being successful enough to warrant a third entry. That entry is set to be released on November 11, 2026, featuring the next step in the prequel story set decades before Jackson's original trilogy. At the end of The Rings of Power season 2, Sauron has control of the Nine Rings of Men, teasing the introduction of the Nazgûl and the War of the Elves and Sauron.

Prime Video's The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power is back with its third season. Here's when to look out for new episodes this month.

Meanwhile, the elves fled north of the fallen Lindon, setting up the formation of Rivendell as a stronghold for the resistance against the Dark Lord. In The Rings of Power season 3, a time skip will see the War of the Elves and Sauron in full swing, as the villain looks to craft the One Ring, and the politics of the island kingdom known as Númenor reach a boiling point. The eight episodes of season 3 will release across three weeks: the first four will drop on Prime Video on November 11, followed by two more on November 18, and the final two on November 25.

Upon the announcement of The Rings of Power, it was revealed that the show's creators had planned out five seasons. As of the conclusion of season 2, this was still the plan, with J.D. Payne and Patrick McKay confirming that development on season 4 is underway. It has been rumored that season 4 will begin production in early 2027, with some reports claiming it will film simultaneously with season 5. Although this remains slightly unclear, it is highly likely that The Rings of Power's initial five-season arc comes to fruition.

The next feature film in the Lord of the Rings franchise will be subtitled The Hunt for Gollum. Andy Serkis, who played Gollum in both Jackson trilogies and was the second unit director on all three Hobbit movies, will both direct and star in the film. Development on the project began in the early 2020s, when Warner Bros. began looking for more routes into the realm of Middle-earth on the big screen. Peter Jackson, Philippa Boyens, and Fran Walsh, the key creative team behind both prior trilogies, were enlisted, leading to the idea of The Hunt for Gollum.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

The Lord of the Rings: The Hunt for Gollum was initially planned for release in the winter of 2026. However, development on the project meant it was pushed to December 17, 2027, with production beginning in New Zealand in July 2026. The story will be told from Gollum's perspective, focused on the period between Bilbo Baggins' birthday party and the Mines of Moria sequence from The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring, in which Gandalf sends Aragorn to search for the creature before Sauron's minions can capture him and find out that the One Ring is in the Shire.

Since production began, cast details have been revealed about The Lord of the Rings: The Hunt for Gollum. Several actors are reprising their roles from the original Lord of the Rings and Hobbit movies, including Ian McKellen as Gandalf and Elijah Wood as Frodo.

Also returning from previous movies is Lee Pace as the elven king, Thranduil. Aragorn, at this point known as Strider, will also return, but Viggo Mortensen is being replaced by Jamie Dornan to represent a younger, less kingly version of the Lord of the Rings character. Joining these cast members are three newcomers in main roles: Kate Winslet, Anya Taylor-Joy, and Leo Woodall. Winslet will play an unknown character called Marigol, while Taylor-Joy plays Seren, an elf serving Thranduil, and Woodall plays Halvard, a Dunedain ranger traveling with Strider.

After development on The Lord of the Rings: The Hunt for Gollum was announced, it was hinted that Jackson, Boyens, and Walsh were looking at developing more feature films in Middle-earth. Shortly before production on that movie began, Warner Bros. announced another Lord of the Rings movie from Stephen Colbert. The TV personality has long been an avid fan of J.R.R. Tolkien, and developed a new story alongside Jackson, Boyens, and Walsh.

With Jackson serving as a producer and Boyens, Colbert, and the latter's son, Peter McGee, as screenwriters, the film was tentatively titled The Lord of the Rings: Shadow of the Past. The title comes from a chapter in The Fellowship of the Ring, with brief details revealing that the film will focus on aspects from the novel that Jackson's film adaptation left out. It will reportedly see the return of Sean Astin, Billy Boyd, and Dominic Monaghan as Samwise Gamgee, Peregrin Took, and Meriadoc Brandybuck, respectively, though it is worth noting this is speculation at this point.

This speculation, however, comes from reports that Shadow of the Past will see the trio recounting their journey out of the Shire, leading into flashback adaptations of the Old Forest section of The Fellowship of the Ring, featuring the character notoriously cut from Jackson's film, Tom Bombadil. As of October 2026, Jackson revealed the film was still in the scriptwriting phase, meaning more updates can be expected going into 2027.

Aside from the aforementioned confirmed projects in development, there will undoubtedly be more Lord of the Rings movies and shows on the way. After all, the franchise is one of the most popular in fantasy history, across movies, TV shows, books, and video games. The success of The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power proves as much, as does the ongoing development of more feature films in the franchise. Beyond that, Peter Jackson has teased even more long-awaited adaptations of Tolkien's work.

Jackson revealed that the rights to further Tolkien works, such as The Silmarillion and The Unfinished Tales of Númenor and Middle-earth, were potentially becoming available. Following the passing of J.R.R. Tolkien's son, Christopher, who was against releasing such rights for adaptation, Jackson revealed that the younger generation now in control of the Tolkien estate was more open to the possibility. This hints that movie or TV show adaptations of both books could be coming in the future.

Among J.R.R. Tolkien's posthumously published Lord of the Rings books is The Silmarillion, a lengthy textbook that covers all the big moments.

If these talks progress further, it would not be a surprise to hear of more Middle-earth projects coming up. Of course, theatrical movies seem to be the focus for those at Warner Bros., even after the recent Paramount Skydance merger. However, TV productions could be possible too. Other big franchises under this umbrella, such as DC and Harry Potter, are receiving massive, big-budget HBO shows to ensure the continuation of both, which could be the case for The Lord of the Rings, along with all the other ways Middle-earth is set to live on.

The Lord of the Rings is a multimedia franchise consisting of several movies and a TV show released by Amazon titled The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power. The franchise is based on J.R.R. Tolkien's book series that began in 1954 with The Fellowship of the Ring. The Lord of the Rings saw mainstream popularity with Peter Jackson's The Lord of the Rings and The Hobbit trilogies.

8 Reasons Young Sheldon Ended With Season 7 (Despite Still Being Really Popular)

📅 Nov 15, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There are several reasons that CBS ended Young Sheldon after season 7 despite its continued popularity. Sheldon said goodbye with a two-episode season finale that aired on May 16th, 2024. The second episode, Memoir, cut back and forth between Sheldon in the timeline of Young Sheldon, and the narrator Sheldon in the future — effectively ending any possibility of a revival for another season. This may have come as a shock, considering that Young Sheldon was a massive ratings hit on CBS, even becoming its number-one comedy for the 2022-2023 season, but its potential ending has long been being discussed.

When The Big Bang Theory ended in 2019, it was also still very popular. CBS wanted to order at least one more season of the show, but Jim Parsons' decision to exit the series as Sheldon Cooper after season 12 basically forced the rest of the cast to wrap up the series. Things are different for Young Sheldon, however, since the main cast was vocal about wanting to continue doing the spin-off. That being said, several elements are not within their control, and that ultimately spelled the end of the family comedy even though it is at the peak of its popularity.

The primary driving force behind Young Sheldon's season 7 ending is The Big Bang Theory's established canon. Because of its prequel nature, the spin-off has a pre-determined end the moment it premiered in 2017. Sadly, the final season was already approaching that point in the timeline. Deviating from the narrative schedule is far too big of a plot hole to ignore. According to The Big Bang Theory lore, Sheldon only has one year left in college when he returns from Germany in Young Sheldon season 7.

After that, he moves to Pasadena to begin his post-grad studies at Caltech. That left the offshoot just enough time to send him off. The fact that Sheldon was leaving the series to go to Germany, and then to Caltech, there was no longer a story to tell when it came to this specific character. When Sheldon's story ends, this series ends, but that does not mean the end for the Young Sheldon universe.

The idea for Young Sheldon came from CBS' attempt to further capitalize on the popularity of The Big Bang Theory, and there was no better way to do that than by awarding its most recognizable character his own show. Sheldon's penchant for sharing anecdotes about his childhood in The Big Bang Theory became the launching pad for the narrative, resulting in a prequel that is set in Texas.

In the last couple of years, however, Young Sheldon has clearly outgrown its premise. Instead of Sheldon being the focal point of the series, it has diversified its storytelling and increased its focus on the rest of the Coopers. The end of Sheldon's story, though, does not mean the end of the Cooper family's story. While there were complaints about the network canceling Young Sheldon from the cast, the complaints were short-sighted because things do not end with the end of Young Sheldon.

When it debuted, Young Sheldon was a hit right out of the gate. CBS was understandably thrilled about this, awarding the show multi-year renewals. In 2021, the network gave the prequel a rare three-year order, guaranteeing its run until the 2023-2024 TV season. Moving beyond that timeframe means that everyone involved would have to get back to the negotiating table for brand-new contracts. It seemed that CBS didn't want to deal with what potentially could be a rigorous process.

However, the spin-off shows that this is only a minor consideration. While Iain Armitage is leaving the franchise, at least as a full-time star, the franchise continues on with a sequel series. And while Armitage will not be returning, at least two of the cast members are coming for the spinoff series, because it centers on Georgie and Mandy. Other actors from the series are also expected to return for the spinoff, as it would make little sense to introduce an entirely new cast of characters for this story. This does mean new contracts, but CBS was willing to do so thanks to the franchise's popularity.

CBS kicked off Young Sheldon with Sheldon and Missy both nine years old. This matched with Iain Armitage and Raegan Revord's real-life age at that time. However, knowing full well that the show can only run until Sheldon is 14 years old, which is when he begins his post-graduate studies in California, the network has slowed down the characters' aging process.

After seven years, Sheldon and Missy are just about to turn 14 in Young Sheldon season 7, when both Armitage and Revord have already turned 15 in 2024. The age difference has become more noticeable in season 6 and was even a point of criticism. This will also be resolved with the spin-off about Georgie and Mandy, as they are both old enough that their ages don't matter as much, and they won't go through as many visible changes as they age. This might mean less screen time for Reagan, however, characters like Meemaw could always still return as well.

Back in 2022, Young Sheldon released its 100th episode, which helped in securing higher rates for syndication. At this point, the show was already in constant re-runs, with the series entering syndication in local markets, covering roughly 90% of the United States back in September 2021. Subsequently, it entered off-network syndication on TBS. Considering this, Warner Bros. Television can continue shopping the show with a lucrative deal now that it is already well past 100 episodes.

By the time the show ended, it was sitting at 141 episodes and those will play on for a long time thanks to the syndication deals. This also will play into the spinoff series. As long as Young Sheldon is playing in constant reruns, fans can watch it (and The Big Bang Theory) and then follow along with the Georgie and Mandy series that takes place between those two franchise shows. Young Sheldon's 100th episode revealed that Georgie and Mandy were unexpectedly pregnant.

Young Sheldon has kept The Big Bang Theory universe alive since the nerd-centric sitcom ended its run in 2019. Despite gradually breaking away from its parent show, Chuck Lorre and Bill Prady's franchise remains relevant years after its mothership show wrapped up thanks to the success of the prequel. Knowing full well how popular the universe is, Georgie and Mandy's First Marriage became the next spinoff that expanded the franchise's long-running tenure.

There were jokes on The Big Bang Theory about Georgie's failed marriages. With him marrying the older Mandy in the Young Sheldon final season, fans knew from TBBT that their relationship was doomed, but it appears that may not be the case if the title means they get married more than once. Going into its third season in 2026, Georgie & Mandy has seen Mary Cooper (Zoe Perry), Meemaw (Annie Potts), and even Missy (Reagan Revord) appear in the spinoff and keep their roles going after Young Sheldon.

Aside from being part of CBS' primetime programming, not to mention being in syndication, Young Sheldon is also accessible through streaming services. It is currently available to watch on demand on Paramount+ like its parent series. Beyond that, however, it is also available on Netflix outside of the US. In 2021, the streaming giant has included the family comedy in its catalog for Australia, Canada, and the United Kingdom.

Given this, WarnerMedia can keep the show relevant and profitable so that it doesn't really have any real motivation to continue Young Sheldon beyond season 7. Of course, there will be plenty of chances for the franchise universe to continue on. Not only are Georgie and Mandy getting their own show, but there are still plans to make another spinoff from The Big Bang Theory itself, possibly for exclusive Max streaming. Ending Young Sheldon allows Warner to expand the series and still make money off this one on streaming.

CBS spent years dancing around Sheldon's story about catching his dad in bed with another woman, and understandably so. Despite how he was painted in The Big Bang Theory, George is actually a decent dad in the prequel, and staying true to canon will drastically change the usually lighthearted show. While it can get away with ignoring the affair, Young Sheldon couldn't escape George's death, which took place when Sheldon was 14.

It would have been difficult for the show to move forward without the Cooper patriarch. Ending it with a shortened Young Sheldon season 7 allowed the network to end on a high note but not push on too much further after Sheldon lost his dad. That is also a nice reason to switch to George and Mandy's First Marriage. Georgie showed up on The Big Bang Theory, and he clearly had a grudge against Sheldon for leaving for brighter pastures while he had to stay home and support the family. That makes it important to leave Young Sheldon for the new story.

The Young Sheldon finale perfectly encapsulated the show and helped wrap up some plot holes fans noticed. The finale saw the George Cooper funeral and his family starting to move on from this great loss. It also showed Sheldon leaving home for Caltech, ending his story and this series in general. It also had a flash forward that explained that an adult Sheldon Cooper was writing his memoir. The plot holes were not really holes at all. They were what Sheldon described to Amy as his "memories." He wasn't going to bury his dad in his memoir.

The Young Sheldon finale proved what many of the cast members have said since CBS announced it was canceling the popular sitcom series. Almost nine million people tuned in to watch the finale, with the funeral and Sheldon Cooper's journey to his "real home" at Caltech. In the Screen Rant review of the Young Sheldon, the best part of the series "is the Coopers. The way George's death and the subsequent funeral is handled is a masterclass in balancing drama and humor." The show ended in the best way it could, considering the limitations.

While there are undoubtedly some fans who would have liked to see Young Sheldon continue, ending at season 7 was the perfect finishing point for the show. This is evidenced by the response to the finale, but even if season 7, episode 14, "Memoir" hadn't been such a fitting send-off, Young Sheldon wouldn't have gained anything by continuing with season 8. The TBBT spinoff had reached the natural conclusion of its narrative regardless of whether the final episode was a hit or flop, and there are a few reasons.

Firstly, as Sheldon told Amy in the flash-forward at the end of "Memoir", Young Sheldon was the adult Jim Parsons recollecting his childhood. Arguably, the death of George Sr. was when, from Sheldon's perspective, his childhood ended. It was a pivotal moment for his character, not just because he lost his father, but also because he lost his innocence. Sheldon clearly saw the death of his dad as the point that his life entered its next phase.

Following losing George Sr., Sheldon was no longer a child, but a teenager on his way to becoming a young adult. While the show could have continued and followed Sheldon all the way through adolescence, this would have gone against the central premise in many ways. Teenaged Sheldon would be an interesting story, but it's not the one that Young Sheldon was created to tell. Yes, Sheldon was technically a teenager since he was 14 years old when Young Sheldon ended, but the death of George Sr. was the moment that crystallized the fact he was no longer a child as far as his own perceptions of his life go.

The other reason for Young Sheldon season 8 being a bad idea was that, by the end of season 7, the rest of the Cooper household were far more pivotal to the show than Sheldon himself. The spinoff of The Big Bang Theory had outgrown its premise as a prequel to another show, and the new characters introduced needed room to grow without being constrained by the need to keep their narratives tied to Sheldon or his adulthood. This is why the next spinoff, George & Mandy's First Marriage, is the logical way to extend the story of the Coopers. It will allow the characters Young Sheldon introduced to flourish in their own right, away from Sheldon or the events of TBBT.

All in all, season 7 was the perfect point to end Young Sheldon. The show became almost as popular as The Big Bang Theory itself, and as far as ratings and viewership are concerned, it could easily have continued on to season 8 and beyond. However, this wouldn't have done justice to its story or the characters it introduced. Parting ways with Sheldon to tell more stories of the Cooper family in a show dedicated to characters Young Sheldon introduced is the most fitting way for the Texas-based prequel of The Big Bang Theory to continue.

A spinoff of the sitcom The Big Bang Theory, Young Sheldon follows the youth and coming-of-age of Sheldon Cooper during his childhood in Texas as he pursues science and academia. The show also follows his parents, siblings, and Mee-Maw, painting a picture of the world where Sheldon grew up.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Shameless' Gallagher Family Tree Explained

📅 Nov 06, 2023  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

The members of the Gallagher family on Showtime's Shameless are a complicated bunch, and their family tree is even more complicated. Adapted from the UK version of the same name, Shameless (US) follows the shenanigans of the Gallagher family in the South Side neighborhood of Chicago during the 2010s. The series spanned a whopping eleven seasons from 2011-2021, equaling the run of its UK predecessor.

Though the Gallagher bloodline expands across generations, the Shameless series focuses primarily on the chaotic head of the family, Frank Gallagher, and his many offspring, as they try to stay afloat in the face of poverty, addiction, and mental illness. Everywhere they turn, the Shameless characters seem to get into trouble: getting too drunk, getting arrested, or getting into blowout fights with friends, strangers, and each other. But if there's one thing Frank's brood has taught during the best Shameless episodes, it's that Gallaghers are not to be messed with.

Peggy Gallagher, is the paternal grandmother of the Gallagher siblings in Shameless. Played by Louise Fletcher, Peggy is referred to as "Grammy" by her grandchildren, is the mother of Frank Gallagher. Along with Frank and his twin brother, Jerry, she had two other sons, Clayton and Wyatt, with an unnamed man. Frank's stories of his childhood paint his parents as rather abusive, claiming they preferred his brothers over him and treated them better.

At the time Shameless begins, Peggy is still in prison for blowing up a meth lab and killing two people. After she is released for health reasons, she shows up at the Gallagher house and wreaks havoc. She later reveals she has terminal cancer, and is eventually smothered to death with a pillow by Sheila Jackson, upon Peggy's request. She is also one of several Shameless characters in the Gallagher family who don't have an equivalent in the UK version of the show upon which it's based.

Frank Gallagher is the patriarch of the Gallagher family and an integral character throughout Shameless. He is the father of Fiona, Lip, Ian, Debbie, Carl, and Liam (though it is revealed early on in a surprise twist that he is not Ian Gallagher's biological father). Frank also has another daughter, Sammi, with his ex-girlfriend Queenie, though he kept her a secret from his family for years until he needed a liver transplant as a result of his chronic drinking.

Frank is a dysfunctional alcoholic who spends most of his time drinking at the local neighborhood bar, The Alibi Room, or else coming up with new schemes to acquire more money for booze. He is also a neglectful and incompetent father, rarely stepping up to his parental duties unless it serves him. Frank is often putting his children in harm's way, causing them to kick him out of the house and shun him off and on throughout the series. Toward the end of the series, he is diagnosed with dementia. Frank dies of COVID-19 in the Shameless series finale.

Gloria LeRoy as Fake Ginger Gallagher, AKA Aunt Ginger, on Shameless

Ginger Gallagher is Frank's paternal aunt, the sister of Frank's father and the Gallagher sibling's grandfather, Mr. Gallagher. Ginger passed away from a cocaine overdose 12 years before the events of Shameless. However, Frank never told his family or the authorities that Aunt Ginger died. Instead, he buried her in the backyard of the Gallagher home (which Ginger owned) so he could keep the house and continue cashing her social security checks.

When the state comes looking for Ginger, the Gallagher's "borrow" a senile elderly woman from the seniors' home where their neighbor and friend, Veronica, works, to pose as Ginger, to avoid losing their house. Ginger is later declared legally dead with a fake body Fiona stole from the same nursing home. While not much is revealed about Ginger, it's implied (especially due to the fact she died of a cocaine overdose) that she was a Gallagher through and through, and was likely quite similar to Frank.

Patrick Gallagher is Frank's paternal cousin. Played by Brent Sexton, Patrick only appears in two seasons of Shameless, appearing in season 3 and season 6. In the season 3 episode "When There's A Will", Patrick got into several confrontations with Fiona over ownership of the Gallagher home, and even manages to trick her into handing over ownership and making "Cousin Patrick" the Gallagher family's landlord.

Patrick then appears again in the season 6 episode "Going Once, Going Twice", and it's here that he shows he's just as bad as Frank when ti comes to managing finances, as he took out a substantial loan on the family home which he has no intention to paying back. Patrick has two sons, Desmond and Warren. Patrick is disliked by the rest of the Gallagher's after he beats them to forging Ginger's will, in which he grants himself ownership of her house, where the Gallagher's live.

Monica Gallagher is the ex-wife of Frank Gallagher and the mother of Fiona, Lip, Ian, Debbie, Carl, and Liam Gallagher. Monica is a character whose absence from her children's lives is just as impactful on the events of Shameless as the times when she's present. She and Frank have an interesting relationship, to say the least, as while the pair are often at each other's throats, there are also plenty of times they give in to temptation and attempt to rekindle old feelings.

Monica abandons her family before the events of the show take place, leaving her eldest daughter, Fiona, to pick up the slack. She is diagnosed with bipolar but rarely takes her meds. Despite her faults, she seems to have good intentions but always ends up disappointing her family with her empty promises. Upon her final visit, Monica remarries Frank before dying of a brain hemorrhage.

Not all of Frank's children count themselves as Gallagher siblings, as he also has an estranged daughter who first appears in Shameless season 4. Sammi Slott is Frank's eldest daughter and only child with Queenie Slott, born several years before Fiona and the rest of Frank and Monica's children. Abandoning Queenie is, ironically, once of the most self-aware things Frank ever did, since he left because he felt he wouldn't be a fit father (which, as any of the Gallagher siblings can attest to, is 100% accurate).

Because she didn't find out he was her father until later in life, Sammi isn't able to see Frank's true colors right away, blinded by her fantasy of having a perfect father-daughter relationship at last. This delusion is why she is the only one of Frank's children to take care of him while he is dying of liver failure. She ends up in prison for trying to shoot Mickey Milkovich, after he accidentally attempted to kill her.

Chuckie Slott is Sammi's only son and Frank's eldest grandchild. He is around 12 years old when he's first introduced in Shameless season 4, and audiences find him living with Sammi in a trailer (though, in season 5, he moves in with Sheila, and finds his way to his grandmother's house afterward in season 6).

Played by Kellen Michael, Chuckie is one of the minor recurring members of the extended Gallagher family, though his appearances have been somewhat memorable, especially since they always seem to reveal something about characters like Frank or Carl. Chuckie is shown to be naïve and not very bright, which makes him an easy target for his uncle Carl to take advantage of for his drug deals. After he is released from juvie for running heroin with Carl, Chuckie eventually goes on to live with his maternal grandmother, Queenie.

Fiona Gallagher is Frank's eldest child with Monica, and his second-eldest daughter overall. While Emmy Rossum departed Shameless before the shwo concluded, she was perhaps the second most central character in the earlier seasons alongside Frank. Due to her mother's abandonment and her father's alcoholism and neglect, Fiona has been the primary caretaker of her younger siblings since she was a child.

After years of playing the role of a parent without legal rights, Fiona files for legal guardianship of her five siblings. Ultimately, the judge awards Fiona and Frank joint guardianship of the kids, allowing Frank to keep custody while Fiona is left with the continued responsibility of providing for her family. While their character arcs eventually deviated, Fiona's narrative in the earlier seasons of Shameless somewhat mirrored her UK counterpart.

Fiona had her fair share of ups and downs, from successfully (and unsuccessfully) investing in real estate to doing prison time for accidentally exposing her infant brother Liam to cocaine and breaking parole. Fiona has had several love interests on Shameless, even briefly getting married once and almost tying the knot again shortly after. However, her romantic relationships are often sabotaged by her family duties or her intimacy issues as a result of her tumultuous upbringing. In the Shameless season 9 finale, Fiona decides to leave Chicago and her family behind to finally start a life of her own.

Lip Gallagher is the eldest son of Frank and Monica Gallagher. Lip is highly intelligent and knows it. He's just as book-smart as he is street-smart, but his arrogance tends to cause rifts between him and his loved ones, especially his older sister, Fiona. Lip and Fiona often butt heads over what's best for their family, but they always manage to make up in the end.

Ever the ladies' man, Lip has several love interests on Shameless but has a hard time expressing his true feelings to his girlfriends. He also loves to start fights which often get him in trouble, sometimes with the law. Lip is the first Gallagher child to graduate high school and the only one to go to college, though he gets expelled for drunkenly assaulting campus security. Like his father, Lip struggles with alcoholism. By the end of Shameless, he has one child with his girlfriend, Tami Tamietti.

Ian Gallagher is the second-eldest son in the Gallagher clan, though he is the only child who was not biologically fathered by Frank. It is revealed early on that Ian's biological father is Frank's brother, Clayton Gallagher. Ian is introduced as a disciplined teen boy in ROTC, who is also closeted. Gradually, Ian comes out to all his family members and begins a secret relationship with Mickey Milkovich.

After illegally joining the army as a minor, hijacking a helicopter, and going AWOL, Ian begins showing signs of manic depression. He is formally diagnosed with bipolar disorder, just like his mother, after kidnapping Mickey's baby. He struggles with his diagnosis at first but can adjust eventually. By the end of Shameless, Ian and Mickey are married. Much like Fiona, Ian's character arc mirrors his the UK version of the character much more than many other members of the Gallagher family in the earlier seasons of Shameless.

The 77 Best Red-Carpet Looks of All Time (and What You Can Learn From Them)

📅 Nov 01, 2023  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

What does it mean to truly own the red carpet? It means showing out the way that stars have at the biggest events on the planet for generations now: In the swankiest, swerviest, most spotlight-stealing fashion possible.

Maybe that’s stunting in a disco-tinged jumpsuit like a ruffled John Malkovich at the 2026 Venice Film Festival, suiting up in sumptuous red velvet like Gwenyth Paltrow in 1999, keeping things simmeringly subtle like Frank Ocean’s Prada look at the 2019 Met Gala, or popping out the big lapels on a suede tux like Tom Holland at GQ’s Men of the Year 2021. Whatever your interpretation, the best red-carpet looks need to feel unexpected, look unforgettable, and radiate confidence.

To help jumpstart your own legendary party ensembles, here are 77 of the greatest red-carpet moments ever—and what you can learn from each.

Don’t be afraid to lean into your sex appeal.

As one wise Twitter user once put it, “Colman Domingo always dresses like he's stepped out of a GQ Mag shoot.”

This lush Gucci velvet suit became so instantly legendary that Gwenyth herself recreated the look more than 20 years later.

Frank caused some consternation (though not around these parts) when he showed up to 2019’s camp-themed Met Gala in an understated, decidedly un-camp look. Four years on, though, his outfit remains a great reminder that sticking to the classics—in this case, black-and-white Prada from head to toe—is never a poor choice.

Ruffles have ridges, and so does this suit.

Leaning retro can sometimes go awry on the red carpet, but Stanfield managed to pull the ‘70s look off with funky ease when he pulled this giant-collared, razor-sharp Saint Laurent jumpsuit out for the Oscars.

This outfit looks like it comes with its own walk-in music, and it’s loud.

The right to rock bare-chested suit isn’t something you demand—it’s something you earn.

Keanu is no stranger to high-low tailoring—he’s been known to rock work boots with sharp suits and graphic hoodies with sport coats. But he’s never pulled that trick off better than he did here in ‘93, layering a badass leather motorcycle vest under a roomy three-button number.

Julia Effin’ Roberts in a Big Effin’ Suit. That’s it. That’s the caption.

Wearing Prada, like Wright at the 2023 Golden Globes, is a surefire way to get a lot of compliments. The brand makes you look stylish and smart. Don’t you just want to ask Wright which local art gallery to visit?

Gerber’s disco-ball gown is stunning, but it’s Butler’s silky, sumptuous, pussy-bow blouse—designed by Saint Laurent, as was his exquisitely tailored tux—that utterly steals the show.

A Dietitian Found the Best Creatine Supplements for Women—Starting from $15

📅 Sep 06, 2023  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

We tested and reviewed the best creatine supplements for women.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

As a dietitian, I don’t hand out supplement recommendations like candy. But when it comes to products that work, creatine is one I confidently stand behind, personally use, and recommend to just about anyone. It’s one of the most well-researched, widely used, and reliable performance enhancers for boosting strength, muscular endurance, and mood, according to a 2021 study by Nutrients.

Creatine supports your body’s production of adenosine triphosphate (ATP), the fast-acting energy your muscles depend on during high-intensity effort. While you can get small amounts from food, supplementing is the most effective way to saturate your muscles. Whether you’re training consistently, getting older, or simply looking to preserve lean muscle, creatine can also speed up recovery.

To find the best creatine supplements, I tested a range of products—flavored and unflavored, pure powders, and more complex blends—paying close attention to mixability, how I felt during workouts, and any shifts in energy, strength, or recovery. Some stood out for their simplicity and quality, while others offered smart, functional add-ons. But before choosing one, it’s worth considering the ingredients, dose, type, and third-party testing.

I’m a St. Louis-based dietitian with nearly a decade of experience in nutrition writing and editing. I also have an M.S. in Nutrition and Dietetics from Saint Louis University. After carefully evaluating dozens of creatine supplements, I found these gems. When evaluating, I didn’t just skim the label—I personally tried them, paying close attention to how they fit into a daily routine and who they might work best for. The Women's Health team pulled from their extensive experience, and also consulted a sports dietitian and a certified trainer, to curate this guide.

If you’re looking for a creatine supplement that’s as trustworthy as it is effective, Thorne Creatine is my number one standout. With just one ingredient—five grams of micronized creatine monohydrate per serving—it delivers the clinically supported dose in a high-quality, straightforward formula. Plus, given it’s micronized, the particles are broken down into a finer powder, which may help improve mixing and absorption compared to standard creatine monohydrate.

That said, it’s still creatine and doesn’t fully dissolve in cold liquids. During testing, I noticed a bit of sediment at the bottom of a cold glass, but the solubility was almost perfect when mixed into warm drinks such as coffee. A quick swirl between sips or using room-temperature water helps smooth things out. It’s also completely tasteless, which makes it easy to stir into just about anything, including coffee, juice, or a post-workout shake.Thorne really earns my trust with its NSF Certified for Sport certification. Every batch is rigorously tested for contaminants and banned substances, which is especially important for competitive athletes, but just as reassuring for anyone who values transparency and quality in their supplements. Plus, it’s free of gluten, soy, and dairy. If you want a creatine that’s consistent and easy to work into your daily routine, it’s an excellent choice.Read more: Best Supplements

Sometimes you just don’t want to mess with powders. Maybe you’re in a rush, not a fan of the texture, or just like the straightforward ease of capsules. If that sounds like you, Optimum Nutrition’s Creatine Capsules offer a convenient, travel-ready format that helps you stay consistent without a blender or shaker bottle.

Each serving of two capsules provides two and a half grams of pure creatine monohydrate, so you'll need four per day to hit the research-backed five-gram dose. One quick note: They contain gelatin, so they're not suitable for vegans.

I’ll be honest—these pills are fairly large, about quarter-sized, and four can feel like quite the literal handful. But I don’t mind them. They go down easily and don’t leave any strange aftertaste, which is a bonus compared to some gritty powders. Plus, because there’s no risk of it settling at the bottom of your drink, there’s no questioning if you got your full serving.

If you're still on board, these capsules deliver consistency and convenience. They're also banned-substance tested, which adds a layer of trust for athletes and anyone serious about ingredient safety.

Also available in 100-, 200-, or 300-count bottles, you can find a size that fits your personal needs and budget. So if you’re looking for an easy, portable way to get your creatine in—and don’t mind a few extra capsules—this one's tough to beat.

If unflavored creatine leaves you bored—or worse, skipping your daily dose—Optimum Nutrition’s blueberry lemonade flavor might be the refresh your routine needs. Flavored creatine can make consistency easier, especially if you’re mixing it with water or combining it with other lightly flavored supplements.

When I tested it, I shook a serving into 10 ounces of cold water. A light foam formed at the top, but it didn’t interfere with the drinking experience. The powder dissolved smoothly, with no grit or residue.In terms of flavor, 10 ounces of water made it a little too light for my taste, but dialing it back to six to eight ounces gave it a bolder, slightly sweeter profile. It’s easy to drink on its own and flexible enough to mix with other supplements, though you’ll want to be mindful of potential flavor clashes.One thing to note: there’s no scoop included. Just note that it contains artificial flavors and sucralose.

To be clear, most creatine powders are technically vegan since creatine is synthetically made without animal by-products, according to a 2020 systematic review by the International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health. Momentous Creatine offers third-party testing that goes beyond industry standards.

In my own testing, it blended smoothly in a shaker bottle with no taste or aftertaste, and only had minimal settling at the bottom. It wasn’t bothersome enough to take away from the experience, and the product’s quality more than makes up for any minor mixability quirks. Plus, the scoop has a long handle for easy grabbing without getting powder all over your hands. Inside, you get exactly what you want: five grams of creatine monohydrate per serving without fillers, sweeteners, or artificialflavors. It’s unflavored, ultra-simple, and Informed Sport-certified.The compact size means the powder sits closer to the lid, so scooping can get a little messy if you’re not careful. However, I felt like the smaller container was worth the minor inconvenience. In practice, the fine powder mixes smoothly into water or shakes, leaving just a slight trace of grit at the bottom—not enough to be distracting. Its neutral flavor also means it plays well with other supplements or blends seamlessly into smoothies without changing the taste.For anyone who values trusted testing and a no-nonsense package, Onnit Creatine is proof that great things can come in small containers.

If you travel often, mix your supplements on the go, or just don’t want to dig around for a scoop every day, Promix Non-GMO Creatine single-serve packets make things refreshingly simple. With 30 pre-measured stick packs, you get convenience, consistency, and zero mess—no scooping, spills, or second-guessing your dose.

During testing, the packets were easy to tear open and mix straight into water with minimal effort. I noticed a very slight bit of sediment at the bottom, but no unpleasant taste, making it a smooth, undetectable addition to any drink routine. Like many other options in this guide, each serving delivers five grams of micronized creatine monohydrate from non-GMO sources. While it’s more expensive than the bulk bag (about $0.96 compared to $0.29 per serving), you gain grab-and-go ease and reliable dosing. True to Promix’s standards, this creatine is third-party tested for heavy metals, soy, and gluten, and it’s vegan-friendly. You can even scan the lot number on your box to access lab results, a level of transparency that goes a long way in today’s supplement space.

Bare Performance Nutrition (BPN) has garnered a cult-like following of fitness enthusiasts and influencers—and the efficacy and quality of their creatine proves it’s for good reason. The product is sourced from Creapure, a highly purified form of creatine monohydrate, according to Michael Hamlin, CSCS, a certified strength and conditioning specialist and the founder of Everflex Fitness. Thanks to its purity, this unflavored powder is third-party approved by Informed Sport, making it especially perfect for the everyday exerciser working on strength, power, and performance training. E

“I can actually feel and see when I’ve been taking this powder consistently,” says WH wellness writer Gabrielle Kassel. “My shoulders become more defined, my legs look stronger in my leggings, and I don’t tap out as quickly during high volume squats.” Each container also provides 60 servings, giving you a bang for your buck.

Attention competitors, Klean Athlete Klean Creatine is the best pick for you, says Lexi Moriarity, RDN, a certified sports dietitian and founder of Fueled + Balanced Nutrition. As it turns out, the product name isn’t false marketing—the powder is genuinely pure enough that it earned an NSF Certified for Sport sticker, she says. Each five-gram scoop of this supplement contains no additional ingredients but creatine. There are no artificial colors, flavorings, sweeteners, GMOs, gluten, or other mystery ingredients, which is important if you ever want to participate in a sports competition that performs drug testing.

The powder is also unflavored and dissolves easily, making it perfect for mixing into water for mid-workout hydration or adding to your pre- or post-shake.

Supplements are unregulated by the Food and Drug Administration (FDA)—and that includes creatine. As such, experts say it’s important to consider the following factors and complete your due diligence to find a high-quality product when shopping for a creatine supp. (Reading this article counts!)

Simplicity is often best with creatine. Look for products that list creatine as the sole active ingredient, ideally without added sugars, unless you're choosing a flavored version intentionally. Avoid proprietary blends that don’t disclose exact creatine content per serving.

The recommended daily dose of creatine is a minimum of five grams per day, according to the International Society of Sports Nutrition. This amount is shown to be safe and effective for maintaining creatine saturation within the muscles.

There are several types of creatine available, including monohydrate, citrate, and nitrate, but creatine monohydrate remains the most reliable. It’s the most extensively researched, dependable, effective, and cost-efficient option.

The U.S. supplement industry is largely unregulated, which means that the FDA doesn’t verify supplement marketing claims before products hit the shelves. But third-party testing can help. Look for certifications from organizations like Informed Choice or NSF Certified for Sport, which ensure the product has been tested for purity, potency, and contaminants. This is especially critical for competitive athletes concerned about banned substances.

For most people, taking creatine daily is not only okay, but it’s actually recommended to maintain optimal muscle creatine levels. Consistent daily use supports better performance, recovery, and long-term benefits.

The most common side effect of creatine is short-term water retention, which can lead to temporary weight gain. Taking more than 10 grams at once may also cause digestive issues such as bloating or diarrhea. However, for most people, creatine is well-tolerated when used as directed.

If you have pre-existing kidney or liver conditions, are pregnant or breastfeeding, or are a child or adolescent, it’s important to consult your healthcare provider before taking creatine. For most healthy adults, creatine is safe and effective, but checking in with your doctor can provide added peace of mind and guidance.

HBO’s <em>Harry Potter</em> TV Series: What We Know So Far

📅 Apr 12, 2023  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fifteen years after Harry Potter and the Deathly Hallows: Part 2, a reboot is on its way.

In the immortal words of Cornelius Fudge, “He’s back.”

Fifteen years after young wizard Harry Potter last appeared in the film Harry Potter and the Deathly Hallows: Part 2—and no matter how much vitriol has followed—Warner Bros. Discovery has summoned another incarnation of the Wizarding World saga. But there’s a twist! This time, the story will take the form of a seven-season television series (rather than an eight-film franchise) and will span a full decade. And there’s more! A new cast will fill the old roles made famous by the likes of Daniel Radcliffe, Rupert Grint, Emma Watson, and co.

Here’s a quick breakdown of what we know so far.

If you’ve watched the original film series, then you already know. Zaslav has promised that the reboot will be a faithful adaptation of the seven books in author J.K. Rowling’s series—meaning it will tell the exact same story as the movies, though with greater detail. Each season of the HBO series will cover the material in one of the books, so we can expect seven seasons in total.

Here’s how HBO Max is describing the series: “The stories from each of Rowling’s Harry Potter books will become a decade-long series produced with the same epic craft, love and care this global franchise is known for. The series will feature a new cast to lead a new generation of fandom, full of the fantastic detail, much loved characters and dramatic locations that Harry Potter fans have loved for over 25 years. Each season will be authentic to the original books and bring Harry Potter and these incredible adventures to new audiences around the world, while the original, classic and beloved films will remain at the core of the franchise and available to watch globally.”

The cast is “new,” meaning—thankfully—Radcliffe and friends won’t be expected to undergo any CGI de-aging. Instead, the roles they made famous will go to newcomers Dominic McLaughlin (as Harry Potter), Arabella Stanton (as Hermione Granger), and Alastair Stout (as Ron Weasley).

Of the hunt for these actors, showrunner/executive producer Francesca Gardiner and director/executive producer Mark Mylod released the following statement in May 2025: “After an extraordinary search led by casting directors Lucy Bevan and Emily Brockmann, we are delighted to announce we have found our Harry, Hermione, and Ron. The talent of these three unique actors is wonderful to behold, and we cannot wait for the world to witness their magic together onscreen. We would like to thank all the tens of thousands of children who auditioned. It’s been a real pleasure to discover the plethora of young talent out there.”

The announcement of these new lead actors followed multiple other rounds of casting reveals, starting in April 2025. The company first shared the identities of four adult actors, three of whom had already been reported by Variety and Deadline. They were as follows:

HBO also announced the cast for two smaller but nevertheless essential roles:

On June 9, 2025, nine more cast members were added to the ensemble:

On July 14, 2025, another round of cast members was announced:

On August 19, 2025, the new members of the Weasley family were announced:

On October 30, Deadline announced the addition of five more members to the season 1 cast:

Gardiner and Mylod, who have each worked on Succession, were enlisted for the new series, Variety first reported in June 2024. They are both serving as executive producers, along with Rowling, Fantastic Beasts and Where to Find Them’s Neil Blair, Brontë Film and TV’s Ruth Kenley-Letts, and David Heyman of Heyday Films. Gardiner will also be the writer and showrunner, while Mylod directed some episodes. In addition, Killing Eve writer Laura Neal joined the writers’ room.

The Harry Potter series is confirmed to release its first season—titled, appropriately, Harry Potter and the Philosopher’s Stone—on Dec. 25, 2026, on HBO and HBO Max.

Yes. On March 25, 2026, HBO Max released the first official teaser for the new series. Watch it below.

A second teaser trailer arrived on September 2, giving fans a better look inside life at Hogwarts. The clip opens with the Sorting Hat Ceremony in the Great Hall before the start-of-term banquet. Harry begs the Sorting Hat to not put him in Slytherin, leading to his Gryffindor placement (and a warm welcome from the rest of his housemates).

“Mr. Potter, I expect you to work hard. Find what sparks your interests, and make solid friendships,” Professor McGonagall tells Harry during a meeting in her office, which is spliced between scenes of him receiving his class schedule and finding the humor in Herbology class. But it isn’t an easy transition for The Boy Who Lived—he struggles in Charms classes, leading to his first set of insecurities. “Everyone thinks I’m this great wizard, but I really don’t know anything,” he says.

Harry later hits his stride as a Quidditch star, giving fans of the franchise a look inside the game as the famous boy wizard chases the golden snitch in the hopes of winning the match. Fans will also recognize The Burrow, Wizards Chess game, and the scary moment when the trio encounters Fluffy.

On Nov. 14, 2026, HBO released the first full scene from its upcoming Harry Potter series, offering a new look at the Hogwarts start-of-term banquet. The 90-second clip follows Harry, Ron, and Hermione as they attend their first feast in the Great Hall after the Sorting Ceremony.

The scene opens with students chanting for headmaster Albus Dumbledore, before Harry asks Ron whether Dumbledore is “a bit mad.” Ron replies, “Yep, totally off his rocker.” Dumbledore then magically conjures the banquet with a snap of his fingers.

The footage also includes a conversation between Dumbledore and Professor Minerva McGonagall about Harry’s placement in Gryffindor. McGonagall remarks on how much Harry resembles his father, James Potter, prompting Dumbledore to remind her, “Looks can be deceiving, Minerva. His nature is entirely his own.”

Although fans would love to see the original members make a cameo in the remake, Radcliffe told ComicBook.com in July 2023 that he won’t be pursuing an opportunity to be in the show. “My understanding is that they’re trying to very much start fresh, and I’m sure whoever is making them will want to make their own mark on it and probably not want to have to figure out how to get old Harry to cameo in this somewhere,” he said. “So I’m definitely not seeking it out in any way. But I do wish them, obviously, all the luck in the world, and I’m very excited to have that torch passed. But I don’t think it needs me to physically pass it.”

Radcliffe also told ScreenRant in February 2026 that he wants the young actors in the new series to be given a chance to succeed on their own terms.

“When these kids got cast, there was a whole thing around the internet [with] people saying we have to ‘look after these kids,’” he told the outlet, when asked what advice he’d give the new actors. “If everyone really means that one of the things you can do for me is not ask about us—me, Emma [Watson], Rupert [Grint]—all the time,” he added. “I would like not to be a weird spectral phantom in these children’s lives and just to let them get on because it’s going to be a new thing and it’s going to be a different thing.”

Without digging too deep into the Discourse™️, we can summarize two main reasons why some audiences aren’t convinced a Harry Potter reboot is, shall we say, necessary:

One is that Rowling will be involved not only as the source material’s creator but as an executive producer, meaning she’ll have some creative control over the reboot and will, of course, benefit financially from its success. That’s not good news for many fans who consider her controversial history of trans-exclusionary feminism to be a rot in an otherwise beloved franchise.

The second is that the story is far from new, nor is it particularly old. Harry Potter is practically inescapable in modern life. Whether it’s his books or his films or his merchandise or his theme parks or his exhibits or his video games, the boy with the lightning bolt scar is astoundingly difficult to avoid. A certain air of exhaustion (not to mention anger) has permeated even the most devoted corners of the Wizarding World fan base. What more can really be squeezed out of not just the same franchise, but the same story? Still, as time has passed since the announcement, it would seem more and more fans have come around to the idea of a Harry Potter adaptation that is deeply and thoroughly faithful to the books. As one commenter on the teaser trailer put it, “This is what I have always wanted. The books fleshed out in [a] TV series. No movie can do justice to how much there is in the books.”

Addressing the conversation around Rowling herself in a presentation timed to the series’ initial announcement, Casey Bloys, chairman and CEO of HBO & Max Content, had only this to add: “J.K. is an executive producer, and her insights will be helpful. We are in the Harry Potter business. The TV show is new and exciting, but we’ve been in the Harry Potter business for 20 years; this isn’t a new decision. We’re comfortable being in the Potter business. J.K. is a very online conversation…it’s very nuanced and complicated and not something we’re going to get into. Our priority is what’s onscreen. The Harry Potter story is incredibly affirmative and positive about love and acceptance, and that’s our priority, what’s on the screen.”
